Environ Epigenet · 2026-11-01
The intergenerational and transgenerational effects of miRNAs in fertility, obesity and stress responses.
Tang YC, Lei KH, Wong ESW, …, Lai KP
▾

Abstract

MicroRNAs (miRNAs) are key post-transcriptional regulators that play pivotal roles in diverse physiological and pathological processes. They primarily act by inhibiting protein translation and promoting degradation of messenger RNA (mRNA), thereby inducing post-transcriptional regulatory effects. Most miRNAs can travel through blood, in extracellular vesicles, and between maternal and fetal tissues. When altered miRNAs were present in germ cells, this induces epigenetic inheritance, which influences embryonic development after fertilization. Aberrant miRNA expression is associated with a wide range of human diseases. This review article will summarize current evidence on how miRNA dysregulation contributes to transgenerational effects on reproduction, metabolism, and stress-related phenotypes. This research focuses on the impacts of miRNAs on reproductive impairment, obesity, and stress-related phenotypes, and highlights evidence that these epigenetic changes can persist across multiple generations. Relevant studies were identified mainly through PubMed database using "miRNA" and "transgenerational effect", with a third keyword according to the topic of interest, including "reproduction", "hormones", "spermatogenesis", "obesity", "high-fat diet", "diabetes", "stress", and "therapy". In reproduction, environmental pollutants (including endocrine disruptors) can alter miRNA expression, impairing gonadal development, reducing primordial germ cells, inducing granulosa cell apoptosis, and disrupting sex hormone synthesis. miRNA-dependent pathways also affect spermatogenesis, sperm function, and oocyte development. In obesity, miRNAs regulate adipogenesis by modulating signaling pathways and transcription programs that control fat-cell formation. Paternal high-fat diet and gestational diabetes can shift miRNA profiles, raising offspring risk for obesity and metabolic disease. Chronic stress changes miRNA expression in germ cells and embryos, shaping stress reactivity and social behavior in later generations. Prenatal maternal stress is linked to lasting psychiatric vulnerability in offspring, with effects potentially persisting across multiple generations without continued stress. Overall, miRNA dysregulation can impair reproduction, metabolism, and stress responses, and these effects may be transmitted transgenerationally via epigenetic mechanisms.

生殖能力、肥満、ストレス応答におけるmiRNAの世代間および世代を超えた影響
マイクロRNA（miRNA）は、多様な生理学的および病理学的プロセスにおいて重要な役割を果たす主要な転写後調節因子です。miRNAは主にタンパク質翻訳を阻害し、メッセンジャーRNA（mRNA）の分解を促進することで、転写後調節効果を誘導します。ほとんどのmiRNAは、血液中、細胞外小胞内、そして母体組織と胎児組織の間を移動することができます。生殖細胞に異常なmiRNAが存在すると、エピジェネティックな遺伝が誘導され、受精後の胚発生に影響を与えます。miRNAの異常発現は、幅広いヒト疾患と関連しています。本総説では、miRNAの調節異常が、生殖、代謝、およびストレス関連表現型に対する世代を超えた影響にどのように寄与するかについての最新の知見を概説します。この研究は、miRNAが生殖障害、肥満、およびストレス関連の表現型に及ぼす影響に焦点を当て、これらのエピジェネティックな変化が複数世代にわたって持続する可能性があるという証拠を強調しています。関連する研究は、主にPubMedデータベースで「miRNA」と「世代間影響」を使用し、関心のあるトピックに応じて「生殖」、「ホルモン」、「精子形成」、「肥満」、「高脂肪食」、「糖尿病」、「ストレス」、「治療」などの3番目のキーワードを使用して特定されました。生殖においては、環境汚染物質（内分泌かく乱物質を含む）がmiRNAの発現を変化させ、生殖腺の発達を阻害し、始原生殖細胞を減少させ、顆粒膜細胞のアポトーシスを誘導し、性ホルモン合成を阻害する可能性があります。miRNA依存性経路は、精子形成、精子機能、および卵母細胞の発達にも影響します。肥満においては、miRNAは脂肪細胞形成を制御するシグナル伝達経路と転写プログラムを調節することにより、脂肪形成を制御します。父親の高脂肪食や妊娠糖尿病はmiRNAプロファイルを変化させ、子孫の肥満や代謝性疾患のリスクを高める可能性がある。慢性的なストレスは生殖細胞や胚のmiRNA発現を変化させ、後の世代のストレス反応性や社会的行動を形成する。出生前の母親のストレスは子孫の精神疾患に対する脆弱性の持続と関連しており、ストレスが継続しなくてもその影響は複数世代にわたって持続する可能性がある。総じて、miRNAの調節異常は生殖、代謝、ストレス反応を損なう可能性があり、これらの影響はエピジェネティックなメカニズムを介して世代を超えて伝達される可能性がある。

Acta Vet Scand · 2026-10-30
Curcumin extract addition to bull semen does not adversely affect in vitro fertilization.
Cojkic A, Sajecka O, Sjunnesson Y, Morrell JM
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: Curcumin extract has demonstrated antioxidant and antibacterial properties in bull semen samples at a concentration of 5%. Nevertheless, its impact on fertilization outcomes remains largely unexplored. This study aimed to evaluate if curcumin extract added to bull semen had an effect on in vitro fertilization (IVF) results. Bovine ovaries were collected from an abattoir, cumulus-oocyte complexes (COCs) were aspirated, graded, and randomly assigned to treatment groups before maturation. The COCs were matured for 22 hours and fertilized using semen from one bull with verified fertility. Semen samples were thawed and treated with 5% curcumin extract for 30 minutes at room temperature, and IVF was conducted at a reduced sperm concentration (0.5 × 106/mL) to better assess treatment effects. Cleavage was evaluated, 44 hours post-fertilization, and embryo development was assessed on day 8. Sperm quality parameters were evaluated using Computer-Assisted Sperm Analysis for motility and kinematics, and Flow Cytometry for viability markers, including membrane integrity, mitochondrial membrane potential (MMP), and chromatin integrity. RESULTS: No statistically significant differences were observed between groups for any IVF outcomes. A trend toward significance was observed for total motility and immotile spermatozoa, with higher motility and lower immotility in the curcumin-treated group compared to controls. CONCLUSION: The results demonstrated that curcumin treatment does not seem to have an effect on in vitro fertilization (IVF) outcomes since no statistically significant differences were observed between the curcumin-treated and control groups in cleavage rate, the proportion of oocytes reaching more than two cells, or blastocyst formation on day 8. These findings indicate that, under the experimental conditions of this study, curcumin does not impair the fertilization capacity of bull sperm.

クルクミン抽出物を雄牛の精液に添加しても、体外受精に悪影響はない。
背景：クルクミン抽出物は、5%濃度で雄牛の精液サンプルにおいて抗酸化作用および抗菌作用を示すことが報告されている。しかしながら、受精結果への影響については、ほとんど研究されていない。本研究は、クルクミン抽出物を雄牛の精液に添加した場合、体外受精（IVF）の結果に影響があるかどうかを評価することを目的とした。屠畜場から牛の卵巣を採取し、卵丘細胞複合体（COC）を吸引、等級分けした後、成熟前に無作為に処理群に割り付けた。COCは22時間成熟させ、受精能力が確認された雄牛1頭の精液を用いて受精させた。精液サンプルを解凍後、室温で30分間5%クルクミン抽出物で処理し、処理効果をより正確に評価するために、精子濃度を0.5×10⁶/mLに下げてIVFを実施した。受精後44時間で卵割を評価し、8日目に胚の発育を評価した。精子の質パラメータは、運動性と運動学についてはコンピュータ支援精子分析、膜完全性、ミトコンドリア膜電位（MMP）、クロマチン完全性などの生存マーカーについてはフローサイトメトリーを用いて評価した。結果：いずれのIVF結果についても、グループ間で統計的に有意な差は認められなかった。総運動精子と不動精子については有意傾向が認められ、クルクミン投与群では対照群と比較して運動性が高く、不動性が低いという結果であった。結論：クルクミン投与群と対照群の間で、卵割率、2細胞期を超える卵母細胞の割合、8日目の胚盤胞形成率に統計的に有意な差が認められなかったことから、クルクミン投与は体外受精（IVF）の結果に影響を与えないことが示された。これらの結果は、本研究の実験条件下では、クルクミンが雄牛精子の受精能力を損なわないことを示している。

Front Plant Sci · 2026-10-30
Multidimensional molecular strategies for crop photoperiod adaptation and latitudinal expansion.
Zhao H, Zhang S, Yin X, …, Bao Y
▾

Abstract

The traditional regional distribution of agriculture was historically constrained by crops' inherent dependence on local environmental cues, particularly photoperiod and temperature. Natural domestication and artificial selection have progressively reduced this dependence on native ecosystems, resulting in a profound restructuring of global agricultural patterns. From the perspective of this global restructuring, this study systematically examines the multidimensional molecular mechanisms underlying photoperiodic adaptive evolution, including population-level point mutations, epigenetic modifications, structural variations, and copy number variations. In parallel, we propose that the latitudinal adaptive expansion of crops represents not merely an ecological niche shift, but a complex evolutionary process driven by coordinated genetic reprogramming across the genome, epigenome, and transcriptome. Consequently, reshaping future agricultural landscapes requires moving beyond the constraints imposed by individual climatic factors and establishing a multidimensional framework that integrates environment, genetic traits, and ecological adaptation. Moreover, rather than focusing solely on the speed of regional expansion, future molecular design should promote the coordinated improvement of broad-spectrum latitudinal adaptability and elite yield-quality traits, thereby securing global food safety and agricultural sustainability.

作物の光周期適応と緯度拡大のための多次元分子戦略
農業の伝統的な地域分布は、作物が本来的に地域の環境要因、特に光周期と気温に依存していることによって歴史的に制約されてきました。自然栽培化と人為選択によって、この在来生態系への依存度は徐々に低下し、世界の農業パターンは大きく再構築されました。この世界的な再構築の観点から、本研究では、集団レベルの点突然変異、エピジェネティック修飾、構造変異、コピー数変異など、光周期適応進化の根底にある多次元分子メカニズムを体系的に検証します。同時に、作物の緯度適応拡大は、単なる生態的ニッチシフトではなく、ゲノム、エピゲノム、トランスクリプトームにわたる協調的な遺伝子再プログラミングによって駆動される複雑な進化プロセスであると提唱します。したがって、将来の農業景観を再構築するには、個々の気候要因によって課される制約を超え、環境、遺伝的特性、生態学的適応を統合する多次元的な枠組みを構築する必要があります。さらに、将来の分子設計は、地域的な拡大の速度だけに焦点を当てるのではなく、広範囲の緯度適応性と優れた収量品質特性の協調的な向上を促進し、それによって世界の食料安全保障と農業の持続可能性を確保すべきである。

Reprod Biol · 2026-10-30
Climate change and declining reproductive efficiency in dairy cattle: Global trends and adaptive solutions.
Praveen KS, Srikanth NR, Shwetha HS, …, Sejian V
▾

Abstract

Climate change is progressively limiting reproductive efficiency in dairy cattle, which has significant consequences for productivity, profitability and global food security. Increasing ambient temperatures, increased temperature-humidity index (THI), recurrent heat waves and climatic variability interfere with reproduction via interconnected endocrine, metabolic and cellular pathways. Heat stress affects the activity of the HPA and HPG axes, leading to increased cortisol levels, altered gonadotropin secretion, disruption of follicular dynamics and compromised luteal progesterone support. At the cellular and molecular levels, oxidative stress, mitochondrial dysfunction, heat shock protein responses and genomic-epigenetic alterations diminish oocyte competence, compromise sperm integrity and hinder early embryonic development, often resulting in embryonic loss and pregnancy failure. While Bos indicus and other climate-adapted genotypes show improved thermotolerance, there remains significant variability among individuals regarding reproductive resilience. This review combines global and regional evidence to clarify the mechanisms behind climate-induced reproductive decline and assesses adaptive strategies such as heat-abatement management, nutritional interventions, timed artificial insemination (TAI), assisted reproductive technologies, genetic selection for heat tolerance and precision monitoring. These insights collectively offer a framework for maintaining dairy reproductive performance considering future climate scenarios.

気候変動と乳牛の繁殖効率低下：世界的な動向と適応策
気候変動は乳牛の繁殖効率を徐々に低下させており、生産性、収益性、そして世界の食料安全保障に重大な影響を及ぼしています。気温の上昇、温湿度指数（THI）の上昇、頻発する熱波、そして気候変動は、相互に関連する内分泌系、代謝系、細胞系の経路を介して繁殖を阻害します。熱ストレスは視床下部-下垂体-副腎（HPA）系と視床下部-下垂体-性腺（HPG）系の活動に影響を与え、コルチゾール値の上昇、ゴナドトロピン分泌の変化、卵胞動態の乱れ、黄体期プロゲステロン分泌の低下を引き起こします。細胞レベルおよび分子レベルでは、酸化ストレス、ミトコンドリア機能障害、ヒートショックプロテイン応答、ゲノム・エピジェネティックな変化が卵母細胞の機能低下、精子の完全性の低下、初期胚発生の阻害につながり、しばしば胚の喪失や妊娠失敗を引き起こします。ボス・インディカス種をはじめとする気候変動に適応した遺伝子型は耐熱性が向上しているものの、繁殖能力に関しては個体間で大きなばらつきが見られる。本レビューでは、世界規模および地域規模の証拠を統合し、気候変動による繁殖能力低下のメカニズムを解明するとともに、暑熱対策、栄養介入、人工授精（TAI）、生殖補助技術、耐熱性遺伝子選抜、精密モニタリングといった適応戦略を評価する。これらの知見を総合的に活用することで、将来の気候シナリオを考慮した酪農繁殖能力の維持に向けた枠組みを提供する。

Plant Cell · 2026-10-28
Promoter variation in BraHDAC1 modulates stomatal regulation and thermotolerance in Brassica rapa.
Zheng M, Xin X, Du B, …, Yu S
▾

Abstract

Heat stress severely limits the productivity of Brassica rapa, a key vegetable crop worldwide. Deciphering the genetic basis of thermotolerance is essential for developing climate-resilient cultivars. By integrating genome-wide association studies (GWAS) with bulked segregant analysis (BSA), we identified two single nucleotide polymorphism (SNP1 and SNP2) in the promoter of the histone deacetylase gene BraHDAC1, which underlies natural variation in heat tolerance. These SNPs create a functional ABRE-CE cis-regulatory module exclusively in thermotolerant haplotypes (Hap1 and Hap3), enabling heat-induced transcription via binding of the transcription factor BraAREB1. Enhanced BraHDAC1 activity in turn represses the pectin methylesterase (PME) gene BraPME1 through histone H3K9/K14 deacetylation at its promoter. This repression, together with the coordinated regulation of additional PME and pectin methylesterase inhibitors (PMEI) genes, is associated with altered total PME activity and broader changes in pectin methylesterification, which may contribute to temporal stomatal regulation. This regulation involves early stomatal opening and increased transpiration associated with lower leaf temperature, consistent with enhanced transpirational cooling, followed by later closure to conserve water. Our findings define a BraAREB1-BraHDAC1 regulatory module in which BraPME1 is one functionally validated downstream target, illustrating how a cis-regulatory variation coordinates transcriptional, epigenetic, and cell wall modification responses to heat stress. The SNP and its associated regulatory module provide direct targets for marker-assisted breeding and genetic improvement of thermotolerant B. rapa.

J Ginseng Res · 2026-10-28
Ginseng-derived extracellular vesicles alleviate ulcerative colitis by regulating macrophage glycolytic reprogramming and H3K18la-mediated epigenetic modification.
Zhang Y, Li R, Yang Y, …, Li Y
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: Ulcerative colitis (UC) is a chronic inflammatory bowel disease with limited treatment options due to adverse effects and variable response rates. Plant-derived extracellular vesicles have emerged as promising therapeutic agents owing to their biocompatibility. Although Panax ginseng exhibits anti-inflammatory properties, the role of ginseng-derived extracellular vesicles (GEs) in UC remains unclear. METHODS: GEs were isolated and characterized. The therapeutic effects of GEs were evaluated in DSS-induced UC mice. Single-cell RNA sequencing was performed to profile colonic immune cells. In vitro experiments using RAW264.7 macrophages and Caco-2 intestinal epithelial cells were conducted to investigate macrophage polarization, glycolytic metabolism, histone lactylation, and barrier function. Gut microbiota and short-chain fatty acid (SCFA) profiles were analyzed by 16S rRNA sequencing and gas chromatography. RESULTS: GEs alleviated DSS-induced colitis symptoms, reduced pro-inflammatory cytokines, restored tight junction proteins, and exhibited good biosafety. Single-cell sequencing revealed that GEs remodeled colonic macrophage subsets, reducing pro-inflammatory Ccl7+ macrophages while increasing Pmepa1+ and Vcam1+ populations, and suppressed glycolytic signatures. In vitro, GEs were internalized by macrophages, inhibited LPS-induced M1 polarization, downregulated GLUT1 and LDHA expression, and reduced ATP and lactate production. Mechanistically, GEs specifically decreased H3K18la enrichment at the IL6, TNFA, and INOS promoters, an effect reversed by exogenous lactate. Additionally, GEs restored gut dysbiosis and elevated levels of propionic, acetic, butyric, and isovaleric acids, which correlated with improved disease phenotypes. CONCLUSION: This study demonstrates that GEs alleviate UC by inhibiting macrophage glycolysis, reducing H3K18la-mediated pro-inflammatory gene expression, protecting the intestinal barrier, and modulating the gut microbiota. These results offer new experimental support for GE-based UC therapy and establish a basis for mechanistic and translational follow-up studies.

J Vis Exp · 2026-10-28
Profiling Active Regulatory Elements in Archival Mouse Tissue by Formalin-Fixed Paraffin-Embedded Cleavage Under Targeted Accessible Chromatin.
Lill E, Dinh E, Dhami M, …, Leichter SM
▾

Abstract

The standard for long-term storage of tissue samples is formalin fixation and paraffin embedding (FFPE), a method that allows samples to be archived at room temperature for decades. However, RNA degrades during storage, and prolonged exposure to ~4% formaldehyde damages RNA and DNA, limiting the utility of RNA sequencing for expression profiling. Our Cleavage Under Targeted Accessible Chromatin (CUTAC) adaptation of CUT&Tag for in situ open chromatin mapping provides high-resolution, genome-wide profiling of FFPE samples, including sections from decades-old tissue blocks. Here, we demonstrate a step-by-step on-slide FFPE-CUTAC protocol, using an antibody to RNA Polymerase II Serine-5 phosphate (RNAPII-S5p), which marks the transcriptional machinery at active regulatory elements when it is paused just before productive elongation, thereby capturing a proxy for active transcription. This workflow makes epigenomic profiling simple and affordable, opening the door to profiling archival samples for clinical applications, retrospective studies, basic transcriptome research, and broader translational research.

Animals (Basel) · 2026-10-27
Blood DNA Methylation Responses to Seasonal Environmental Variation in the Giant Panda (Ailuropoda melanoleuca): A Whole-Genome Bisulfite Sequencing Analysis of Multiple Individuals Across Multiple Time Points.
Liu J, Ma R, Bi W, …, Qi D
▾

Abstract

Seasonal environmental variation is a widespread stressor for wildlife and can induce plastic changes in DNA methylation. Blood DNA methylation is an integrative molecular indicator of overall physiological status, yet its seasonal dynamics in the giant panda remain unclear. This study aimed to profile genome-wide blood methylation patterns in giant pandas under natural field conditions and to reveal the epigenetic strategies underlying their seasonal adaptation. Twelve blood samples were collected from three wild-trained giant pandas in the Daxiangling Nature Reserve during winter (n = 6) and summer (n = 6) between 2023 and 2025 and were subjected to whole-genome bisulfite sequencing. The genome-wide mC/C ratio (4.18-4.98%) was dominated by the CG context. Winter-summer comparison identified 7113 differentially methylated regions and 3063 differentially methylated genes. These genes were enriched in transcriptional regulation, cell surface receptor signaling, miRNA metabolism, and MAPK, Ras, and T/B cell receptor signaling pathways. Winter-hypermethylated genes were enriched in both immune defense and energy-consuming processes, whereas winter-hypomethylated genes showed limited enrichment, indicating that hypermethylation-mediated suppression dominates seasonal adaptation. This study reveals, for the first time, a seasonal methylation fingerprint in giant panda blood characterized by a "summer immune enhancement, winter energy conservation" pattern. As sampling occurred in natural habitats, these differences reflect integrated epigenetic responses to temperature, photoperiod, food availability, and pathogen pressure rather than single-factor effects. These findings provide molecular evidence for seasonal adaptation in endangered mammals and candidate markers for captive panda management and wild-release assessment.

Biomolecules · 2026-10-27
Heat Stress and Bull Semen: From Endocrine and Molecular Dysregulation to Targeted Intervention.
Zhu Q, You W, Cheng H, …, Song E
▾

Abstract

Heat stress, a critical environmental stressor intensified by global warming, increasingly threatens bull semen quality. This review synthesizes the multi-level mechanisms underlying this impairment. Heat-stressed bulls exhibit behavioral changes that serve as early indicators of reproductive dysfunction, alongside pronounced declines in routine semen parameters and alterations in the seminal plasma biochemical microenvironment. At the endocrine level, heat stress disrupts the hypothalamic-pituitary axes, suppressing testosterone synthesis and perturbing the testicular microenvironment to compromise spermatogenesis. At the molecular level, altered expression of heat-stress-related genes and epigenetic reprogramming-involving non-coding RNAs, DNA methylation, and m6A methylation-collectively disturb this process. Notably, the "gut-testis" axis emerges as a key pathway: heat-induced gut microbiota dysbiosis compromises intestinal barrier integrity, and immune and endocrine signals from translocated microbial metabolites ultimately impair testicular function and semen quality, as evidenced by cross-species studies. At the application level, the above mechanistic evidence, which is largely derived from model animals or non-bovine livestock, still requires direct and systematic validation in bulls. Collectively, this review provides an integrative mechanistic framework for heat stress-induced deterioration of bull semen quality and highlights critical directions for developing targeted mitigation strategies under global climate change.

Metabolites · 2026-10-27
Lactate-Driven Biphasic Reprogramming of Macrophage Immune Responses.
Guo Q, Ren P, He L, …, Fang C
▾

Abstract

Lactate is a metabolic substrate, an extracellular signaling molecule, and a precursor for protein lactylation, with diverse effects on macrophage immunity. Evidence from infection, tissue injury, efferocytosis, and cancer indicates that lactate-associated pathways can amplify inflammation, support resolution and tissue repair, or sustain immune suppression. These outcomes depend on the timing, cellular source and compartment, chemical formulation and pH, transport and receptor engagement, and the chemistry and targets of lactylation. In this narrative review, we critically synthesize the biochemical foundations of lactate metabolism and lactyl-group donation and examine the mechanisms underlying these distinct macrophage responses. We distinguish model-specific biphasic responses from a universal sequence of early inflammation followed by resolution, and separate reparative programs from impaired antimicrobial or antitumor immunity. We also evaluate evidence for histone and non-histone lactylation, emphasizing the limits of metabolic perturbations and chromatin associations for establishing site-specific causality. This framework identifies experimental variables that should be controlled and supports context-selective investigation of lactate-related therapeutic targets in inflammatory diseases and cancer.

Guo Q, Ren P, He L, Gao J, Lu R, Wang Y, Zhang Z, Han X, Zhang X, Fang C
5
Int J Mol Sci · 2026-10-27
Mitochondrial Metabolic Reprogramming and Immune Evasion in Colorectal Cancer: Research Advances and Mechanistic Perspectives.
Ding D, Hu J, Wang Y, Jiang L
▾

Abstract

Colorectal cancer (CRC) is one of the most prevalent malignancies of the gastrointestinal tract worldwide. Notably, most patients with microsatellite-stable (MSS) tumors show primary resistance to immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs), creating an unmet clinical need. The mechanisms underlying immune evasion and strategies to overcome therapeutic resistance have therefore become important topics in CRC immunology research. Recent studies indicate that mitochondrial metabolic reprogramming contributes to immune evasion in CRC. Tumor cells display metabolic plasticity through dynamic shifts between glycolysis and oxidative phosphorylation (OXPHOS), which can intensify the depletion of critical nutrients, particularly glucose and glutamine, within the tumor microenvironment (TME). These metabolic changes can also promote the accumulation of immunosuppressive metabolites, including lactate, thereby impairing the function of effector cells such as CD8+ T cells and dendritic cells (DCs) and supporting the expansion of regulatory T cells (Tregs) and myeloid-derived suppressor cells (MDSCs). Lactate may further reshape the immune landscape through hydroxycarboxylic acid receptor 1 (HCAR1) signaling and histone lactylation. In parallel, mitochondrial quality control can regulate tumor immunogenicity by influencing mtDNA availability and cGAS-STING signaling. Therapeutic approaches targeting glycolysis, glutamine metabolism, fatty acid oxidation, lactate transport, and mitochondrial dynamics have shown immune-sensitizing activity in preclinical models. Nanodelivery systems and gene-editing technologies may improve the spatial and cellular specificity of these interventions. This review summarizes how mitochondrial metabolic reprogramming contributes to immune evasion in CRC and evaluates mitochondria-directed strategies for improving treatment responsiveness, particularly in MSS CRC.

DNA Methylation-Mediated Regulation of Plant Adaptation to Abiotic Stress.
Liu G, Wang H, Tang Y, …, Jiang Z
▾

Abstract

Climate change is intensifying environmental stresses, including drought, high temperature, and salinity, posing increasing challenges to plant growth and development. In addition to genetic variation, DNA methylation is an important epigenetic mechanism that regulates gene expression without altering the underlying DNA sequence through the addition of methyl groups to the fifth carbon of cytosine. Genome-wide dynamic changes in DNA methylation and demethylation at CG, CHG, and CHH sites contribute to the regulation of stress-responsive transcriptional networks, thereby shaping plant phenotypes and facilitating the establishment of stress memory. In recent years, advances in methylome profiling and molecular validation have shifted research from describing DNA methylation changes to elucidating their regulatory mechanisms. In this review, we summarize the molecular mechanisms underlying the establishment, maintenance, and removal of DNA methylation, with a particular focus on its dynamic changes and regulatory roles in plant responses to cold, heat, drought, and salt stresses. It also compares the characteristics of DNA methylation responses across different types of stress and discusses their potential molecular mechanisms. Overall, DNA methylation represents an important regulatory layer in plant responses to abiotic challenges, and its dynamic changes are closely associated with plant adaptation, providing new insights into the mechanisms underlying stress adaptation.

Unraveling pyrethroid-induced multisystem toxicity in fish: a review of pathways, drivers, and countermeasures.
Xu M, She S, Yang S, Fei H
▾

Abstract

Pyrethroid pesticides, widely applied in agriculture and public health, frequently contaminate aquatic ecosystems and pose substantial risks to teleost fish due to their lipophilicity and bioaccumulation potential. This review synthesizes recent advances in understanding pyrethroid-induced multisystem toxicity in teleosts, covering neurotoxicity via voltage-gated sodium channel disruption, cardiovascular impairment, reproductive and endocrine disruption, immunotoxicity, and metabolic dysfunction. Oxidative stress emerges as a common mediator across these effects. Toxicity is modulated by abiotic factors (temperature, salinity, sediment organic matter), biotic factors (species, developmental stage, sex), and co-exposure with other contaminants. Detoxification strategies include physical/chemical remediation, microbial biodegradation, and nutritional or immunomodulatory interventions that enhance endogenous antioxidant defenses. Despite progress, critical knowledge gaps remain regarding chronic low-level exposure, metabolite toxicity, transgenerational epigenetic effects, and climate-change interactions. Future research should integrate molecular, organismal, and environmental data to develop predictive risk assessment frameworks and field-applicable mitigation measures, ensuring sustainable pyrethroid use while safeguarding fish health and aquatic biodiversity.

Abstract

Here, we demonstrated that Tirzepatide (TZP) can significantly ameliorate echocardiographic indicators of pulmonary vascular load and attenuate distal pulmonary vascular remodeling by suppressing PASMC phenotypic switching. Vibrio parahaemolyticus in a Changing World: A One Health Perspective on Epidemiology, Antimicrobial Resistance, Virulence, and Climate-Driven Risks.
Sanchez-Rojas IC, Bonilla-Aldana DK, Solarte-Jimenez CL, …, Rodriguez-Morales AJ
▾

Abstract

Vibrio parahaemolyticus is a halophilic, Gram-negative bacterium widely distributed in marine and estuarine environments and recognized as a leading cause of seafood-associated gastroenteritis worldwide. In recent decades, its global emergence has been driven by environmental changes, particularly ocean warming, as well as intensified aquaculture and the globalization of seafood trade. Representative epidemiological data illustrate this burden: V. parahaemolyticus was detected in 3.11% of 79,075 diarrheal samples across 11 Chinese provinces and in 8.1% of 6951 acute-diarrhea samples in eastern China, while Zhejiang Province documented 383 outbreaks involving 4382 cases between 2010 and 2022. This narrative review provides a comprehensive overview of the epidemiology, virulence factors, antimicrobial resistance, and One Health dimensions of V. parahaemolyticus. The pathogen's ability to cause disease in humans largely depends on key virulence determinants, including thermostable direct hemolysin (TDH), TDH-related hemolysin (TRH), and secretion systems that facilitate host colonization and cytotoxicity. From a One Health perspective, V. parahaemolyticus represents a critical interface among environmental, animal, and human health, and also causes severe diseases in aquaculture, including acute hepatopancreatic necrosis disease (AHPND) and translucent postlarval disease (TPD) in shrimp, as well as vibriosis in bivalve mollusks such as oysters, where infection may result in tissue damage and substantial larval and juvenile mortality, with important economic consequences. The increasing emergence of antimicrobial-resistant strains further complicates clinical management and highlights the role of environmental reservoirs and aquaculture practices in disseminating resistance. Additionally, climate-driven changes in temperature, salinity, and marine ecosystems are expanding this pathogen's geographic distribution and altering its seasonal dynamics. Strengthening integrated surveillance systems, improving food safety practices, and promoting interdisciplinary research, including studies of the molecular mechanisms underlying interactions between the epigenome and V. parahaemolyticus, are essential to mitigate the growing risks associated with V. parahaemolyticus. A comprehensive One Health approach is critical to address its evolving impact on global public health and food security.

Dobrzyńska MM, Jankowska-Steifer EA
▾

Abstract

Over the past decades, male reproductive health has markedly declined, as evidenced by reduced sperm concentration, impaired motility, increased morphological abnormalities, and a higher incidence of testicular disorders. Environmental factors, particularly endocrine disruptors (EDs), are increasingly recognized as important contributors due to their ability to interfere with hormonal signaling and cellular homeostasis. Mechanistically, EDs induce oxidative stress, mitochondrial dysfunction, DNA damage, endocrine imbalance, and epigenetic reprogramming. These alterations include changes in DNA methylation, histone modifications, and non-coding RNA expression that may persist beyond the period of direct exposure and potentially affect subsequent generations. This part of review also discusses emerging strategies to mitigate ED-induced reproductive toxicity, including antioxidants, natural bioactive compounds, probiotics, and other interventions targeting oxidative stress, apoptosis, and endocrine dysfunction. Collectively, these findings highlight the complex molecular, epigenetic, and endocrine mechanisms underlying ED-induced male reproductive dysfunction and its potential long-term consequences.

Hypoxia-induced epigenetic reprogramming of cancer stem cells: Linking stemness, metastasis, and therapy resistance.
Al-Khreisat MJ, Narzikulov R, Allela OQB, …, Bainsal N
▾

Abstract

Hypoxia is a defining feature of the solid tumor microenvironment and a central driver of cancer-stem-cell (CSC) biology, yet the epigenetic mechanisms linking oxygen deprivation to stemness, metastasis, and therapy resistance remain fragmented across the literature. This review synthesizes that evidence into a unified mechanistic framework. Beyond the canonical hypoxia-inducible factor (HIF)-1α/HIF-2α switch, hypoxia directly reprograms the epigenome through oxygen-dependent histone demethylases and DNA dioxygenases (TET, JmjC-domain enzymes), establishing chromatin as a parallel, HIF-adjacent oxygen sensor. This crosstalk spans DNA methylation dynamics that gate HIF binding while HIF reciprocally reshapes DNMT/TET expression; histone modifications that redistribute genome-wide and are selectively co-opted at pluripotency loci; and histone lactylation, a metabolically-derived mark we position as an emerging fourth epigenetic pillar - though, as detailed below, its mechanistic demonstration within defined CSC populations remains more preliminary than for the other three layers. We trace how these layers converge on the core pluripotency circuitry (OCT4, SOX2, NANOG, KLF4, MYC), critically examine the limited evidence for a "hypoxic memory" outlasting reoxygenation - currently demonstrated only in bulk tumor and circulating-tumor-cell populations, not defined CSCs - and connect these mechanisms to EMT, circulating-tumor-cell survival, and multi-modal therapy resistance. We distinguish mechanisms demonstrated directly in CSCs from those inferred from general cancer contexts, identifying histone lactylation in CSCs and true post-reoxygenation memory as areas needing further investigation.

Osteoinductive Microgrooved Membranes for Alveolar Ridge Preservation.
Wang J, Yang Y, Meng L, …, Zhang W
▾

Abstract

Tooth extraction is commonly followed by alveolar ridge resorption, and conventional barrier membranes act largely as passive barriers that do not actively promote early bony sealing of the socket crest. This study asked whether a barrier membrane redesigned to spatially organize early reparative cells at the socket crest preserves alveolar ridge dimensions. Gelatin methacryloyl (GelMA) membranes bearing V-shaped microgrooves were reinforced with electrospun polycaprolactone (PCL) fibers, and recombinant human bone morphogenetic protein 2 (rhBMP-2) was immobilized by stepwise photocrosslinking. Using primary macrophages and bone marrow-derived mesenchymal stem cells, as well as mouse, rat, and beagle models, we assessed macrophage localization and polarization, hypoxia signaling, lactate metabolism, histone lactylation, bone regeneration, and ridge preservation by micro-computed tomography, histology, immunofluorescence, Western blotting, and quantitative reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction. Early bone regeneration in rat calvarial defects was the primary efficacy outcome, and ridge preservation in beagles was a translational outcome. In exploratory analysis, microgrooves guided early myeloid cell enrichment across the defect and were associated with a localized hypoxic microenvironment, lactate dehydrogenase A-dependent lactate production, histone H3K18 lactylation, and a reparative macrophage phenotype that was reversed by lactate dehydrogenase A inhibition in vitro. At 7 and 14 d, microgrooved membranes showed earlier progression of defect healing and extended new bone toward the defect center. Immobilized rhBMP-2 enhanced regeneration to achieve near-complete calvarial closure within 2 wk. In beagles, central socket healing was more advanced at 2 wk in the sites treated with rhBMP-2 microgrooved membranes . At 3 mo, treated sites retained alveolar ridge width, height, and the buccal bone plate relative to natural healing. Microgrooved barrier membranes combined with localized osteoinduction increased early bone regeneration in a controlled rodent comparison and provide proof of concept for alveolar ridge preservation in a canine extraction model.

Previously Unrecognized Congenital Hypogonadotropic Hypogonadism in an 89-Year-Old Man: A Case Report.
Ferreira M, Saraiva PO, Nunes F, …, André ME
▾

Abstract

Congenital hypogonadotropic hypogonadism (CHH) is a rare disorder typically recognized during adolescence because of absent or incomplete pubertal development. We report the case of an 89-year-old man admitted for an ischemic stroke whose physical examination revealed a high-pitched voice, markedly sparse facial and axillary hair, bilateral gynecomastia, a markedly reduced penile size clinically suggestive of micropenis, and nonpalpable testes in the scrotum. Hormonal evaluation showed a total testosterone concentration below 7.00 ng/dL, luteinizing hormone below 0.07 IU/L, and follicle-stimulating hormone of 0.83 IU/L, consistent with severe central hypogonadism. Ultrasonography demonstrated both testes within the inguinal canals with reduced testicular size. Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) of the sellar region showed no structural hypothalamic-pituitary abnormality. Although advanced age and acute systemic illness may have contributed to further functional suppression of the hypothalamic-pituitary-gonadal axis, these factors could not account for the developmental genital abnormalities. Integration of the phenotypic, biochemical, and imaging findings supported a clinical diagnosis of previously unrecognized CHH. This case illustrates an exceptionally late recognition of CHH and highlights the diagnostic value of developmental phenotypic features in identifying a congenital disorder that may remain unrecognized for decades.

▾

Abstract

Mikania micrantha, a highly invasive weed, faces constraints on its northward range expansion in South China due to low winter temperatures. Although DNA methylation is increasingly recognized as a mediator of plant stress adaptation, it remains unknown whether epigenetic regulation couples cold-stress tolerance with the production of allelopathic secondary metabolites in invasive plants. Here, we comprehensively characterized genome-wide DNA methylation dynamics and their transcriptional consequences in germinating embryos under three temperature regimes (14 °C, 25 °C, and 30 °C) by integrating whole-genome bisulfite sequencing and RNA-Seq. Exposure to 14 °C significantly delayed germination without arresting the process. Integrative analysis identified 1103 differentially methylated region-associated genes, of which 434 were also differentially expressed. Because this is an integrative methylome-transcriptome association study without metabolite quantification or functional validation, these results are consistent with the hypothesis that cold-induced epigenetic reprogramming is associated with transcriptional changes in terpenoid biosynthesis, which may contribute to antioxidant and membrane-protective functions under chilling stress; the possible accumulation of allelopathic sesquiterpene lactones and any role in maintaining competitive advantage during cold seasons remain working hypotheses that require experimental validation. The results of this study offer new epigenetic perspectives and supporting data for the management and prevention of M. micrantha invasion.

Exposomic Determinants in Heart Failure Among Older Adults - Mechanistic Links, Clinical Evidence, and Future Perspectives.
Hahad O, Münzel T, Zheng J, …, Kuntic M
▾

Abstract

PURPOSE OF REVIEW: Heart failure (HF) in older adults emerges from the interplay between biological aging and the totality of environmental and behavioural exposures conceptualized as the exposome. Traditional cardiovascular risk factors and intrinsic cardiac pathology only partially explain HF burden, as chronic exposures to air pollution, traffic noise, temperature extremes, toxic metals and chemicals, artificial light at night, and adverse social environments, along with low greenness and walkability, substantially influence HF incidence, decompensations, and prognosis in older patients with multiple comorbidities. RECENT FINDINGS: Recently, epidemiological studies have associated many of the exposomic factors with HF incidence and morbidity. Age‑related impairments of the myocardium and vasculature make them particularly vulnerable to external stressors. Mechanistically, aging, HF, and environmental factors converge on shared pathways including, oxidative stress and mitochondrial dysfunction, inflammaging, impaired autophagy and proteostasis, genomic and epigenetic alterations, cellular senescence, dysregulated neurohormonal and circadian signalling, and fibrosis with maladaptive remodelling. Through these mechanisms exposome chronically accelerates cardiovascular aging and lowers the threshold for HF onset and progression. This review considers a shift from an organ‑centred view of HF in older adults to a systems‑level, life‑course perspective that explicitly incorporates exposomic determinants, and argues that reducing harmful exposures while promoting green space, physical activity, walkability, and healthy dietary patterns should be integral to HF prevention and management strategies in aging societies.

▾

Abstract

Macrophages are essential for myelin clearance following peripheral nerve injury, yet the mechanisms linking their functional heterogeneity to metabolic regulation remain unclear. Abstract

Hum Reprod Open · 2026-10-23
In vitro incubation of human sperm does not change DNA methylation.
Kjøller ET, Dumargne MC, Ingerslev LR, …, Barrès R
▾

Abstract

STUDY QUESTION: Is DNA methylation in mature spermatozoa affected by different culture media? SUMMARY ANSWER: Incubation of mature spermatozoa in hydrogen peroxide (H₂O₂) or palmitate for 24 h did not alter the DNA methylome. WHAT IS KNOWN ALREADY: Children conceived through ART face a higher risk of disorders linked to abnormal DNA methylation. As culture media can modulate DNA methylation in somatic cells, abnormal DNA methylation after ART may be caused by factors present in culture media during sperm incubation. STUDY DESIGN SIZE DURATION: Sperm from four donors underwent targeted shotgun proteomics analysis, focusing on protein size ranges that include epigenetic modifying enzymes. Additionally, sperm samples from 15 men were incubated for 24 h in either standard media, media with H₂O₂, or media containing palmitate, two media known to induce acute DNA methylation changes in somatic cells. DNA methylation profiles of these incubated sperm samples were then compared to those of pre-incubation sperm using Reduced Representation Bisulfite Sequencing (RRBS). PARTICIPANTS/MATERIALS SETTING METHODS: Sperm samples were obtained from 15 healthy Caucasian men aged 20-40 years. Participants using prescription medications daily or with a history of testicular disease were excluded. Motile sperm were isolated through the swim-up technique. Protein analysis was conducted via in-gel digestion followed by mass spectrometry. DNA methylation was evaluated using RRBS and validated for select genomic regions with targeted bisulfite pyrosequencing. MAIN RESULTS AND THE ROLE OF CHANCE: Proteomic analysis returned 1930 proteins, including 144 newly identified proteins. Consistent with previous studies, we confirmed the presence of DNA methyltransferase 1 (DNMT1), while de novo methyltransferases DNMT3A and DNMT3B were not detected. Incubation in control, H₂O₂, and palmitate media all led to decreased sperm motility, with the decline occurring the fastest under the H₂O₂ condition. Sperm DNA methylation remained unchanged across all three culture conditions. LARGE SCALE DATA: N/A. LIMITATIONS REASONS FOR CAUTION: RRBS analysis covers only a portion of the genome, so DNA methylation changes in unexamined regions cannot be excluded, particularly at repeated regions of the genome. Moreover, since this study tested only three culture media conditions, it remains possible that other media formulations could impact DNA methylation. WIDER IMPLICATIONS OF THE FINDINGS: This study indicates that the DNA methylome of mature human spermatozoa is stable despite the presence of DNA methylation machinery components. These results imply that DNA methylation changes in response to environmental stress are more likely to happen during earlier stages of spermatogenesis. While the various culture media used in ART are unlikely to alter the DNA methylation patterns of individual sperm cells, the possibility that certain sperm with specific methylation signatures are selectively favored cannot be ruled out. The Novo Nordisk Foundation Center for Basic Metabolic Research is an independent research center at the University of Copenhagen, partially funded by an unrestricted donation from the Novo Nordisk Foundation (NNF18CC0034900). This work was supported by the French Government (National Research Agency, ANR) through the 'Investments for the Future' programs LABEX SIGNALIFE ANR-11-LABX-0028-01 and IDEX UCAJedi ANR-15-IDEX-01. DISCLOSURES: R.B. is a recipient of research funding granted by the Novo Nordisk Foundation. E.A. is a shareholder of ExSeed Health Ltd.

Mol Biol Rep · 2026-10-23
Dissecting polycystic metabolic ovarian syndrome heterogeneity (PMOS): multi-omics, AI subtyping, and precision management.
Pradhan S, Gopalakrishnan AV
▾

Abstract

Polycystic metabolic ovarian syndrome (PMOS) is the most common endocrine disorder, affecting 8-12% of women of reproductive age and increasing the risk of infertility, type 2 diabetes, and heart disease. However, traditional symptom-based diagnostic criteria do not fully capture the underlying biological diversity of PMOS. Recent research using multi-omics and artificial intelligence (AI) has revealed that PMOS is best understood as a syndrome with multiple subtypes. Data-driven clustering of clinical variables consistently identifies four major subtypes: HA-PCOS, OB-PCOS, SHBG-PCOS, and LH-PCOS. Each subtype shows unique hormonal, metabolic, and genetic features, including distinct patterns in lipid, amino acid, and energy metabolism. Immune system disruptions and impaired blood clot breakdown in the ovarian environment are also notable features. Novel findings suggest that altered lactate metabolism and changes in histone lactylation may contribute to granulosa cell dysfunction, although further studies are needed to confirm these mechanisms. Machine learning models that integrate clinical, metabolic, and genetic data have achieved high diagnostic accuracy and can provide interpretable results. Potential targeted therapies, such as GLP-1 receptor agonists, SGLT2 inhibitors, N-acetylcysteine, and NK3R antagonists, have been proposed for specific subtypes, but have yet to be validated in clinical trials. Remaining challenges include small and homogeneous study cohorts, limited long-term data, and barriers to care in low-resource settings. Overall, recognizing PMOS as a syndrome with distinct subtypes highlights the promise of AI-powered, personalized approaches for more effective and equitable management.

Phytohormonal networks and thermomemory in plant heat stress tolerance: From mechanistic integration to precision engineering.
Raza A, Li Y, Siddique KHM, …, Hu Z
▾

Abstract

Rising global temperatures pose a major threat to crop productivity, requiring mechanistically informed, scalable strategies to enhance heat stress (HS) tolerance. Phytohormones are central regulators of plant HS responses; however, tolerance instead emerges from dynamic, context-dependent network behavior. This review provides a mechanism-oriented synthesis of how major phytohormones coordinate HS adaptation. Specifically, we (i) reframe phytohormone action through core regulatory modules, including redox control, carbon-energy allocation, reproductive stability, and growth-defense trade-offs; (ii) resolve phytohormonal crosstalk into functional interaction networks that determine stress outcomes; (iii) define the molecular basis of heat priming and thermomemory; and (iv) evaluate translational strategies, including genetic engineering, precision breeding, and advanced delivery systems, together with their limitations under field conditions. We propose a unifying conceptual model in which HS tolerance is an emergent property of hierarchical phytohormonal networks operating across fast signaling, transcriptional memory, and metabolic buffering layers. This framework shifts the focus from single-pathway manipulation toward precision "hormonomics" and network-level engineering, providing a systems-level platform for developing climate-proof crops in a future, hotter climate.

Abstract

Varicocele (VC) is a common correctable factor in male infertility, yet its effect on fertility and response to repair vary markedly among patients. This narrative review examines whether mitochondrial injury links the hemodynamic lesion to oxidative stress, sterile inflammation, and persistent spermatogenic damage. Venous stasis, hyperthermia, hypoxia, and metabolic disturbance can impair mitochondrial respiration, membrane potential, ATP production, and quality control. These changes increase mitochondrial reactive oxygen species and favor mitochondrial DNA (mtDNA) oxidation and release. Oxidized mtDNA can engage cyclic GMP-AMP synthase (cGAS)-stimulator of interferon genes (STING) and NOD-like receptor family pyrin domain-containing 3 (NLRP3) signaling in nucleated testicular cells, but the evidence is uneven. Human studies mainly document sperm mitochondrial dysfunction, oxidative injury, DNA fragmentation, and altered inflammasome-related factors in seminal plasma. The integrated mtDNA-cGAS-STING-NLRP3 sequence is supported chiefly by animal and non-VC mechanistic models and has not been causally demonstrated in human VC. Anatomical and temporal context is therefore critical. Events in testicular somatic, germ, and immune cells during spermatogenesis cannot be inferred from epididymal, ejaculated-sperm, seminal-plasma, or female-tract findings. Endoplasmic reticulum stress and autophagy or mitophagy may modify mitochondrial injury, whereas programmed cell death, blood-testis barrier disruption, and epigenetic alterations may extend its consequences. Most of these links still require cell-resolved and longitudinal validation. Guideline-directed varicocele repair remains the clinical foundation. Sperm DNA fragmentation and other mechanism-related markers are useful research tools but lack source specificity or validated treatment thresholds, and pathway-directed therapies remain preclinical. The distinctive contribution of this review is a compartment-resolved and evidence-graded model that separates demonstrated findings from plausible links. It also defines the experiments needed to determine whether residual mitochondrial-inflammatory injury predicts reproductive outcomes or response to safe adjunctive treatment.

Zhang J, Gao B, Zhang Y, Guo J, Zhong C

Int J Nanomedicine · 2026-10-22
Nanomaterials Reshaping Tumor Metabolism: A New Frontier to Overcome T Cell Exhaustion.
Zhu X, He L, Li J, …, Song S
▾

Abstract

Adoptive antitumor immunity ultimately fails because metabolically hostile tumor microenvironments (TME) drive T cells into a dysfunctional exhausted state that current checkpoint blockade only partially reverses. Crucially, the principal metabolic stresses - accumulation of lactate, lipids and adenosine, deprivation of glucose and amino acids, and hypoxia - do not act in isolation but form a densely cross-regulated network that progressively reinforces T cell exhaustion. This review synthesizes how each barrier arises and how they amplify one another, then maps nanomaterial-based countermeasures onto this network: catalytic nanomaterials that degrade lactate and adenosine, lipid-modulating and mitochondria-targeted formulations, amino-acid replenishment systems, and hypoxia-relieving designs that pair metabolic correction with checkpoint inhibition or photodynamic therapy. Particular attention is given to the design principles - tissue targeting, stimuli-responsive release and rational combination - that determine whether metabolic reprogramming yields durable restoration of T cell function rather than transient phenotypic rescue. Emerging preclinical evidence indicates that nanomaterial-mediated metabolic remodeling can convert the immunosuppressive TME into an immune-supportive niche and recover effector function of exhausted T cells. We further identify the key gaps that currently limit clinical translation: the absence of subset-level evaluation distinguishing reversible progenitor-like exhausted T cells from terminally exhausted cells, the lack of epigenetic endpoints such as histone lactylation, off-target metabolic suppression of T cells themselves, and the manufacturing and safety hurdles of clinical-grade nanomaterials. Resolving these issues would establish metabolic reprogramming as a credible frontier for next-generation cancer immunotherapy.

Anim Reprod Sci · 2026-10-22
GDNF sustains female germline stem cell homeostasis during ovarian aging in laying hens.
Ma Y, Zhang S, Yu C, …, Zhu G
▾

Abstract

Ovarian aging is a major factor contributing to the decline in egg production in laying hens, particularly during the late laying period. Female germline stem cells (FGSCs), which possess self-renewal and differentiation capacities, are considered important for replenishing the oocyte pool and maintaining ovarian function. However, the mechanisms regulating FGSC function during ovarian aging remain largely unknown. In this study, we investigated the role of glial cell line-derived neurotrophic factor (GDNF) in FGSC proliferation. We found that aged hens with high laying performance exhibited significantly greater FGSC abundance and higher ovarian expression of GDNF and its receptors, GFRα1 and RET, than low-laying hens. In vitro experiments further showed that GDNF significantly promoted FGSC proliferation. Transcriptome analysis revealed that GDNF treatment was associated with enhanced PI3K-Akt and mTOR signaling and reduced FoxO- and p53-related signaling, indicating a transcriptional shift toward cell proliferation and survival. Consistently, GDNF increased the expression of multiple proliferation-associated genes and significantly expanded endogenous FGSC populations in ovarian organ cultures. Collectively, these findings demonstrate that exogenous GDNF promotes FGSC proliferation, while higher GDNF expression is associated with greater FGSC abundance in high-laying aged hens. These results suggest that GDNF may contribute to FGSC maintenance by modulating signaling networks related to cell proliferation and survival, providing new insights into the cellular and molecular mechanisms underlying ovarian aging and reproductive decline in laying hens.

Biol Rev Camb Philos Soc · 2026-10-22
Categorisation of female germline stem cells based on their morphology and marker expression profiles.
Sousa RL, León-Félix CM, Bols PEJ, Appeltant R
▾

Abstract

In 2004, researchers found a population of mitotically active germ stem cells in adult mice capable of differentiating into oocyte-like cells, which, by contradicting the long-existing dogma on the non-existence of postnatal oogenesis, caused a shift in fertility research. Further studies conducted in mice, human and other species have continuously confirmed the existence of these female germline stem cells. Given that these cells represent an extremely small portion of the total ovarian cell population, their isolation has been exceptionally challenging, with some studies being unable to identify these cells. Moreover, studies that identified these stem cells have observed a variety of sizes, morphologies and expression profiles. Thus, there is a lack of consensus on the characteristics of female germline stem cells, including their terminology, as different researchers have called them 'female germline stem cells', 'oogonial stem cells', 'egg precursor cells', 'very small embryonic-like stem cells' or other terms. This review summarises the findings on these stem cells from the ovaries of a range of species, with the aim of proposing a standardised terminology based on their intrinsic characteristics. We hope that this terminology will be unanimously used in future fertility studies.

Clin Epigenetics · 2026-10-22
Lactate-driven metabolic-epigenetic reprogramming in breast cancer: immune evasion and therapeutic resistance.
Sun L, Wang L, Zeng W, Zeng S
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: Breast cancer develops within a metabolically heterogeneous tumor microenvironment characterized by regional hypoxia, glycolytic activation, lactate accumulation, extracellular acidification, and impaired antitumor immunity. Lactate is now recognized not only as a metabolic end product, but also as a transported substrate, a receptor ligand, and a potential source of lysine lactylation. MAIN BODY: In this Review, we summarize current evidence on lactate production, MCT1/MCT4-dependent trafficking, GPR81/HCAR1-mediated sensing, immune remodeling, and metabolic-epigenetic regulation in breast cancer. We organize these effects into three interconnected layers. First, extracellular acidification, nutrient competition, and redox stress produce rapid and potentially reversible suppression of CD8⁺ T cells, natural killer cells, and dendritic cells. Second, sustained lactate-rich conditions alter the relative fitness of immune and stromal populations, weakening cytotoxic effectors while supporting regulatory T cells, tumor-associated macrophages, suppressive myeloid populations, and stromal immune exclusion. Third, histone and non-histone lactylation, together with RNA-level and chromatin regulatory mechanisms, may contribute to more durable state fixation. We distinguish direct breast cancer evidence from mechanisms established in other cancers and from transcriptome-based lactylation-associated signatures. We also discuss methodological limitations, subtype-specific evidence, and therapeutic strategies targeting lactate production, transport, sensing, and downstream epigenetic regulation. SHORT CONCLUSION: Lactate-driven metabolic and epigenetic regulation provides a useful framework for understanding immune evasion and therapeutic resistance in breast cancer. However, direct mechanistic evidence remains concentrated in triple-negative breast cancer and tumor-intrinsic phenotypes. Lactylation should therefore be considered a candidate mechanism of durable state fixation rather than a universal explanation for lactate-associated effects. Future studies integrating direct lactylation measurements, spatial multi-omics, functional perturbation, and patient-derived models will be required to define clinically actionable lactate-dependent states.

Effects of cryopreservation on the viability and function of Larimichthys crocea ovarian tissue.
Chen J, Chen M, Sun Z, …, Zhou L
▾

Abstract

Cryopreservation of mature fish eggs and embryos remain major barriers to the preservation of maternal genetic information because of their large size and high yolk content. This study developed a cryopreservation protocol for ovarian tissue from juvenile large yellow croaker (Larimichthys crocea) to preserve oogonial stem cells as an alternative approach. A screening of permeable and non-permeable cryoprotectants, alone and in combination, was performed using a simple two-step freezing procedure (-80°C for 1 h followed by liquid nitrogen immersion). Post-thaw cell viability was assessed by trypan blue exclusion, and the optimal combination of 10% Me2SO, 15% PG, and 15% FBS was identified, yielding the highest viability of 72.12 ± 8.87%. Histology revealed that oogonia and Perinucleolus stage oocytes maintained intact morphology post-cryopreservation, whereas vitellogenic oocytes sustained significant cryodamage, including oolemma shrinkage, vacuolization, and rupture. qRT-PCR results further revealed upregulated germline stem cell markers of vasa, klf4, oct4, and nanos2, alongside downregulated sycp3 and nanog gene, suggesting that the high viability of oogonia, which consistent with the results of histology. In addition, ccnd1, rhoA, and cirbpa exhibited reduced transcript levels and hsp70 showed elevated levels, indicating that cryopreservation is associated with altered expression of genes involved in cell cycle regulation, cytoskeletal signaling, and stress response. Finally, functional validation via intraperitoneal transplantation into 12 dpf larvae confirmed that cryopreserved oogonia retained the abilities to migrate and colonize on the genital ridge at 15 days post-transplantation. The study established cryopreservation protocol of ovarian tissue that can effectively safeguard the oogonial stem cells, providing a feasible approach for conservation of maternal germplasm in L. crocea and serving as a valuable reference for germplasm conservation of other economically important and endangered fish species.

▾

Abstract

Whether female germline stem cells (fGSCs) exist in postnatal mammalian ovaries has been debated for decades. Since the fGSCs were first reported in 2004, a large number of studies have been conducted using different approaches. However, the persistent controversy surrounding the results has caused confusion among many researchers and clinicians. To clearly clarify this question, we developed advanced tracing system to label any possible fGSCs in adult female mice. Kaushik N, Rastogi S, Jain V, …, Kumar N
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: The somatic gonad plays a central role in supporting germline development and maintaining oocyte and embryonic integrity, both of which are essential for sustained reproductive function. With age, deterioration of somatic gonadal structures compromises germline support, leading to declines in oocyte morphology and increased embryonic abnormalities. The insulin/IGF-1 signaling (IIS) pathway regulates longevity and reproductive aging, and its reduction (rIIS) in the daf-2(e1370) mutant extends reproductive span in Caenorhabditis elegans. While prior work has largely focused on germline stem cells and oocyte maturation, the molecular mechanisms by which IIS preserves somatic gonadal architecture and thereby maintains oocyte and embryonic morphological integrity remain poorly understood. Elucidating how reduced IIS influences the somatic gonad can provide insights into the coordination of systemic aging pathways with tissue-specific mechanisms that sustain reproductive health. METHODS: We identified somatic gonad-enriched genes from temporal transcriptomic datasets of reduced IIS animals, highlighting collagens consistently upregulated with age. Using RNAi knockdowns in both systemic and somatic gonad-specific RNAi-sensitive strains of Caenorhabditis elegans, we examined effects on self-reproductive span, oocyte and embryonic morphology, and somatic gonad integrity. Age-associated morphological changes were assessed by differential interference contrast microscopy, and regulatory dependencies were tested via quantitative polymerase chain reaction following knockdown of key transcription factors. RESULTS: Several collagens were found to be essential for maintaining somatic gonadal architecture and preserving oocyte morphology during aging. Gonad-specific knockdown of these collagens caused structural deterioration, impaired oocyte and embryo quality, and accelerated reproductive decline. These defects occurred without a major reduction in total progeny number, suggesting that age-related reproductive loss arises primarily from tissue deterioration rather than immediate fertility impairment. Expression and function of these collagens were largely dependent on key transcription factors downstream of IIS. CONCLUSION: Our findings uncover a previously unrecognized role for somatic gonadal collagens in preserving reproductive function under IIS. By maintaining gonadal architecture and sustaining oocyte and embryonic morphological integrity, these collagens act as downstream effectors of IIS-mediated reproductive longevity. Targeting gonadal collagen networks may therefore represent a potential strategy to mitigate age-related reproductive decline and warrants further investigation in higher-order models.

J Genet Genomics · 2026-10-19
LSD1 exerts crucial functions to drive the progression of meiotic prophase I during spermatogenesis.
Zhu T, Qin S, Dong Y, …, Wang Z
▾

Abstract

Epigenetic modifications are critical determinants governing the proper initiation and progression of gametogenesis. Accumulating evidence has indicated that epigenetic dysregulation underlies the pathogenesis of infertility. However, detailed information on the individual powerful roles of these epigenetic enzymes in facilitating programmed meiosis remains elusive. This study indicates that lysine-specific demethylase 1 (LSD1) plays critical roles during meiotic prophase I in mouse spermatogenesis. Germ cell-specific deletion of the Lsd1 gene in male mice caused meiotic arrest at the pachytene stage, ultimately leading to infertility. In primary spermatocytes of Lsd1-deficient mice, compromised homologous chromosome synapsis and DNA double-strand break repair activated the DNA damage checkpoint pathway, triggering spermatocyte apoptosis. Mechanistically, LSD1 directly binds to the promoters of meiotic genes, including Prdm9 and Rpa2, to regulate their expression during meiosis. PRDM9 and RPA2 are upregulated upon LSD1 loss and are essential for homologous recombination and DNA double-strand break repair. The promoters of Prdm9 and Rpa2 are also occupied by H3K4me2, whose levels increase upon LSD1 loss. Collectively, this study highlights the crucial role of LSD1 in orchestrating meiotic genes, possibly through its demethylase activity during spermatogenesis, and may help explain the pathology of non-obstructive azoospermia in humans.

Spermatogenesis and spermatozoon structure shed light on the reproductive biology of Phoronis ovalis, phoronid species with lecitotrophic larva.
Temereva E, Yurchenko O
▾

Abstract

The structure of spermatozoa, traditionally employed to address phylogenetic and taxonomic questions, appears to correlate with the reproductive biology and lifestyle peculiarities of certain species. Phoronis ovalis is the only known phoronid species that lacks spermatophores and possesses lecithotrophic creeping larvae, which predominantly develop within the maternal tube. Currently, the mechanisms of sperm transfer and insemination in P. ovalis remain unknown. The consecutive stages of spermatogenesis and the ultrastructure of mature spermatozoa in P. ovalis have been investigated using light and electron microscopy. Spermatogenesis does not display marked peculiarities when compared to that in other studied phoronid species and spermatozoa are V-shaped with an acrosome, a nucleus, and a pair of mitochondria in the one limb that is also common to most phoronid species. However, in P. ovalis, the acrosome is notably large in both diameter and length; it contains a prominent internal cistern as well as a network of narrow cisternae filled by material with low electron density, and it is positioned laterally along the nucleus. The mitochondria appear to be larger in comparison to previously studied species of phoronids. Additionally, chains of unfertilized oocytes were observed within the tubes of P. ovalis. This finding, combined with the unusual acrosomal structure, and enlarged mitochondria may indicate that in this species insemination seems to be external and fertilization is more probable within the clutch of eggs inside the tube, rather than within the maternal organism, as is the case in other phoronids.

Abstract

Safflower is an Asteraceae oilseed crop valued for its high-quality unsaturated fatty acids (≈90% oleic and linoleic acids), natural floral pigments, and diverse bioactive compounds. These attributes underpin its applications in functional foods, nutraceuticals, pharmaceuticals, and various industrial sectors. This review consolidates recent advances across major omics domains. Genomics covers chromosome-scale assemblies, GWAS panels, QTL mapping, and diagnostic candidate genes for molecular breeding. Transcriptomics summarizes regulatory networks for flavonoid and oil biosynthesis, stress-responsive transcription factors (NAC, WRKY, bZIP, AP2/ERF), and seed developmental programs. The proteomics section addresses stress-inducible, therapeutic, and storage/lipid metabolic proteins linked to quality and stress adaptation. Metabolomics outlines quinochalcone and flavonoid pathway architecture, color-specific metabolic profiles, and emerging clinical and nutraceutical applications. Phenomics highlights UAV-based high-throughput phenotyping, RGB and multispectral imaging for salt and drought screening, and machine-learning-assisted germplasm authentication. Epigenomics focuses on DNA methylation and miRNA-mediated regulation. Finally, lipidomics and metagenomics consider oil authentication biomarkers, refining optimization, and microbiome-driven nutrient cycling shaping medicinally important compounds. Despite these advances, critical gaps remain in multi-omics integration, CRISPR-based functional validation, genomic selection pipelines, and detailed characterization of root-shoot architecture under combined stresses. Addressing these gaps will advance mechanistic understanding of climate resilience and enable the development of stress-tolerant, high-yielding safflower cultivars with improved oil quality and bioactive profiles, thereby supporting climate-resilient production systems and high-value safflower-based products at scale.

Abstract

Male reproductive health reflects coordinated spermatogenesis, endocrine regulation, reproductive-tract patency, sexual function, and sperm genomic integrity. The evidence map comprised 105 unique sources: 99 PubMed-indexed peer-reviewed sources and six official grey-literature/guideline sources. Google Scholar supported supplementary discovery and citation chaining, and overlapping PubMed records were counted once after de-duplication; no unique Google-Scholar-only journal source remained in the mapped set. Four additional references were used exclusively to guide scoping-review methodology and reporting, giving 109 references in the complete bibliography. The mapped literature identifies endocrine-disrupting chemicals, pesticides, metals, air pollution, occupational heat, selected medicines and gonadotoxic treatments, metabolic and infectious diseases, varicocele, genetic disorders, and systemic illness as relevant reproductive-health contexts, but the strength and causal interpretability of evidence vary substantially. Recurrent mechanisms include oxidative stress, inflammation, endocrine disturbance, mitochondrial dysfunction, vascular impairment, sperm DNA damage, and epigenetic alteration. The review supports couple-centred evaluation, source control of avoidable exposures, treatment of reversible disease, fertility preservation before gonadotoxic therapy, and specialist-led medication decisions.

Cells · 2026-10-16
The Crosstalk Between Metabolic Reprogramming and Histone Methylation in Tumor Immunity and Immunotherapy.
Dou J, Xue R, Gao W, Liang F
▾

Abstract

Histone methylation regulates chromatin structure and gene expression, whereas tumor metabolism provides bioenergetic and biosynthetic inputs. These processes are bidirectionally linked: S-adenosylmethionine supplies methyl groups for histone methyltransferases, α-ketoglutarate(α-KG) supports JmjC-domain demethylases, and D-2-hydroxyglutarate (D-2HG) can inhibit α-KG-dependent dioxygenases. Conversely, histone methylation can regulate metabolic gene expression, as illustrated by KMT2D-dependent enhancer remodeling and glycolytic gene expression in melanoma models. Lactate regulates chromatin through histone lactylation, a modification distinct from histone methylation; where the two intersect, as in the retinoblastoma H3K18la-SUZ12/PRC2-H3K27me3 circuit, the crosstalk is model-specific. This review examines these interactions in tumor cells and the immune microenvironment, emphasizing enzyme specificity, histone mark, cell type and experimental context. We highlight that changes in S-adenosylmethionine or α-KG do not produce uniform effects across enzymes, loci or cell states, and that tumor-associated macrophages are not captured by a fixed M1/M2 metabolic dichotomy. Mechanistic findings, preclinical results and early-phase clinical observations are considered separately; single-arm activity reported for valemetostat in adult T-cell leukemia/lymphoma is presented as activity in a defined cohort, not as an established survival benefit. Finally, we discuss how single-cell and spatial approaches can help resolve cell-type-specific and niche-specific interactions in heterogeneous tumors.

N6-Methyladenosine and 5-Methylcytosine Epitranscriptomic Regulation of Male Reproduction and Fertility.
Salih SM, Al-Hendy A, Yang Q
▾

Abstract

Functional spermatozoa production depends on spermatogenesis, a complex developmental process that requires precise regulation of gene expression at multiple levels. Although considerable progress has been made in understanding the transcriptional and epigenetic mechanisms governing germ cell development, post-transcriptional regulation mediated by RNA modifications has recently emerged as an important component of male fertility regulation. Epitranscriptomics, the study of chemical RNA modifications and their associated regulatory proteins, has revealed diverse roles for RNA modifications in spermatogenesis, sperm maturation, fertilization, and early embryonic development. Among more than 170 RNA modifications identified to date, N6-methyladenosine (m6A) and 5-methylcytosine (m5C) are well characterized in the male reproductive system. Functional studies in genetic knockout animal models show that disrupting key epitranscriptomic regulators contributes to abnormal germ cell development at multiple developmental stages. Notably, adverse environmental exposure reprograms the germ cell epitranscriptome, leading to abnormal spermatogenesis and male infertility. In light of recent advances, this narrative review summarizes current progress in male fertility, with particular emphasis on m6A and m5C RNA modifications. Finally, it outlines future perspectives on RNA modification studies in male fertility. Deeper mechanistic insights into male infertility can contribute to the development of new targeted therapies.

Adv Sci (Weinh) · 2026-10-15
Mitochondrial Quality Control in Degenerative Musculoskeletal Diseases: Signaling Networks and Therapeutic Landscapes.
Gao H, Cheng Z, Chen X, …, Zhang Y
▾

Abstract

Degenerative musculoskeletal disorders, including osteoarthritis (OA), intervertebral disc degeneration (IVDD), and osteoporosis (OP), are increasingly viewed as disorders of mitochondrial quality control (MQC) rather than passive mechanical wear. The MQC network, comprising mitochondrial dynamics, mitophagy-biogenesis coupling, and inter-organelle communication, organizes the aging-inflammation-metabolism axis. Its collapse releases mtDNA fragments that act as damage-associated molecular patterns (DAMPs) activating innate immunity via cGAS-STING, and metabolic intermediates that remodel the epigenome through histone lactylation, potentially locking acute stress into a "metabolic memory", although the mtDNA-methylation component remains hypothesis-level. Multi-omics and cell-free mtDNA liquid biopsy underpin a proposed subtyping into bioenergetic failure, immuno-inflammatory activation, and senescent cell accumulation, still a within-OA hypothesis pending independent and cross-disease validation. Therapy is shifting from symptom management toward organelle medicine, including cardiolipin stabilizers, PROTAC-mediated protein degradation, mitochondrial base editing, and engineered mitochondrial transplantation. Metformin has randomized trial evidence in knee OA pending confirmation, NAD+ precursors are in early clinical testing, and SS-31 holds accelerated approval only for Barth syndrome; other strategies remain preclinical. Delivery barriers and disease heterogeneity require precision stratification, biomimetic models, and AI-driven decision-making. This review delineates the shared molecular logic of the three diseases and proposes a prototype subtyping-to-treatment roadmap pending validation.

Early Postoperative Testicular Volume Ratio Is Associated With Long-Term Growth Failure After Unilateral Redo Orchiopexy in Children.
Baltrak YA, Turker Çolak S, Tutus K, …, Tuncer R
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: Redo orchiopexy is usually evaluated based on the position of the scrotum after surgery, but satisfactory scrotal placement may not necessarily reflect preserved long-term testicular growth. In this study, we evaluated whether the early postoperative testicular volume ratio (TVR) is associated with long-term growth failure after unilateral redo orchiopexy in children. METHODS: This retrospective single-center cohort study included children who underwent unilateral redo orchiopexy between January 2013 and January 2023. Early postoperative ultrasonography was performed 6-12 months after surgery, and subsequent follow-up ultrasonography was performed at ≥24 months. TVR was calculated as the operated testicular volume divided by the contralateral testicular volume × 100. Long-term growth failure was defined as long-term TVR <50% and/or the absence of an interval increase in operated testicular volume beyond expected ultrasonographic measurement variability. The discriminative ability of early TVR was assessed using receiver operating characteristic (ROC) analysis. The ROC-derived cutoff was considered exploratory because it was derived and evaluated in the same cohort. RESULTS: Sixty-eight children met the inclusion criteria. The mean age at redo orchiopexy was 7.8 ± 2.9 years, and the median follow-up was 36 months. Long-term growth failure occurred in 20 patients (29.4%). Children with growth failure had significantly lower early TVR than those with preserved growth (48.6 ± 13.2% vs. 80.4 ± 16.8%, p < 0.001). Early TVR showed good apparent discrimination, with an area under the curve (AUC) of 0.84 (95% confidence interval [CI], 0.72-0.96). In the exploratory multivariable model, each 10-percentage-point increase in early TVR was associated with a lower odds of growth failure (odds ratio [OR] 0.56; 95% bootstrap CI 0.36-0.84). The apparent model AUC was 0.86, and the optimism-corrected AUC after 1000 bootstrap resamples was 0.81. The exploratory 55% cutoff yielded 82% sensitivity, 78% specificity, 69% positive predictive value, and 88% negative predictive value. CONCLUSIONS: Early postoperative TVR is associated with long-term testicular growth failure after unilateral redo orchiopexy. A TVR of approximately 55% may help identify children who require closer ultrasonographic surveillance, but this threshold is hypothesis-generating and requires external validation before clinical implementation. TVR should be interpreted as an imaging-based risk stratification marker rather than a direct measure of endocrine, fertility, or histologic testicular function.

Gene therapy for non-obstructive azoospermia: therapeutic avenues and translational pathway to clinical application.
Jiang MX, Jing T, Feng HL, Zhu Y
▾

Abstract

Non-obstructive azoospermia (NOA) represents the most severe male infertility. Although microsurgical testicular sperm extraction combined with intracytoplasmic sperm injection offers opportunities, approximately 50% of NOA patients still fail to obtain sperm for clinical treatments. Fortunately, the emergence of precision medicine, particularly advances in gene therapy, has opened new therapeutic avenues for NOA in preclinical studies, demonstrating proof-of-concept in animal models. This review examines current progress in NOA gene therapy. Firstly, three principal therapeutic strategies: lipid nanoparticle-mediated mRNA delivery for transient protein replacement, CRISPR-based gene editing for permanent correction, and stem cell-based approaches combined with genetic modification are comparatively analyzed.
