Animals (Basel) · 2026-10-16
The Chornobyl Dogs: Why Scientific Evaluation Must Accompany Population Management.
Tuech JJ, Guillin O, Pinson J, …, Betz JA
▾

Abstract

Nearly four decades after the 1986 reactor explosion, several hundred semi-feral dogs persist within the Chornobyl Exclusion Zone. Genomic work has characterised their population structure and shown that radiation-induced mutation does not, on current evidence, drive the differentiation between groups; the cause of that differentiation remains unresolved, and their epigenome has never been examined. These dogs are a potentially valuable model for chronic, low-dose, multi-stressor exposure, not because they are "radiation-adapted", a contested claim, but because they are a human-adjacent, densely sampled, pedigreed mammal for which comparative canine-human tools already exist. Since 2017, a humane sterilisation programme, carried out at the request of and in cooperation with the Ukrainian authorities, has reached near-complete coverage of the established populations. That programme was justified: dogs are formally prohibited in the zone, they injure workers, free-roaming animals inside an operating nuclear facility raise security as well as veterinary concerns, and reproduction was outpacing what the environment could support. Our argument concerns timing rather than policy. The scientific value of this population was never evaluated while it was still breeding, and the archived material that does exist, roughly 400 dogs including parent-offspring trios and repeat-sampled individuals, was assembled for population genetics rather than for the regulatory questions now worth asking. That archive should be analysed for methylation and expression now, as this is the only access to the population as it was. The animals themselves have not gone: several hundred identified dogs are still alive there, so sampling at veterinary contact and the retention of tissue at natural death or clinically indicated euthanasia remain possible, provided they are planned and funded in advance rather than improvised. More generally, the scientific evaluation of a population should accompany the decision to manage it rather than follow it, because sterilisation is not reversible at the population scale and an archive cannot be assembled retrospectively. We set out what such an evaluation requires at the outset of a programme. Its most concrete element asks nothing of the animals: a sterilisation clinic is the only routine access to a free-roaming population under anaesthesia, and the reproductive tissue it removes-the one material capable of addressing germline questions-is discarded as surgical waste.

Plant Cell · 2026-10-16
A cellular view of seed development and germination.
He C, Auroux L, Lewsey M, Whelan J
▾

Abstract

Seeds are fundamental to global food security, yet their encapsulated structure, dense storage compounds, and spatial heterogeneity have created a "hidden box" problem, often restricting research to bulk and tissue approaches that mask cell type-specific regulatory programs. Advances in single-cell and spatial transcriptomics now enable systematic dissection of seed biology at cellular resolution. Integration of transcriptional profiling with chromatin accessibility and transcription factor binding data has enabled construction of cell type-specific gene regulatory networks for seeds, advancing from correlation to causal inference and identifying master regulators of developmental transitions. These approaches illuminate how early embryonic patterning establishes transcriptional identities, how metabolic zonation coordinates storage compound accumulation and mobilization, how chromatin states maintain dormancy and license germination competence, and how spatiotemporal coordination integrates hormone signaling with energy metabolism across tissues. Single-cell and spatial approaches are establishing seeds not as opaque structures but as assemblies of coordinated cellular programs amenable to systematic analysis and targeted manipulation for crop improvement.

Abstract

The expansion of soybean (Glycine max (L.) Merr.) cultivation into Western Canada is constrained by distinct abiotic stressors, including shorter growing seasons and drought. Heat shock proteins (HSPs) are critical regulators of stress adaptation; however, characterizing their expression in field conditions is often confounded by significant annual environmental variability. In this study, we integrated differential expression analysis, consensus Weighted Gene Co-expression Network Analysis (WGCNA), and rigorous protein-protein interactomic modeling across 4 years of field data (2018-2021) involving 10 genotypes grown in Eastern and Western Canada. Abstract

Plant embryogenesis converts a zygote into a patterned, maturation-competent embryo. Since the major syntheses published in 2019 and 2021, allele-resolved transcriptomics, single-cell and spatial profiling, and three-dimensional imaging have revealed substantial variation in zygotic genome activation and cleavage geometry across angiosperms, challenging models derived solely from Arabidopsis. Abstract

The hypothalamic-pituitary-adrenal (HPA) axis is central to regulating the body's stress response. While HPA axis function enables physiologic and behavioral adaptation to stress, dysregulation of its feedback mechanisms - driven by early-life adversity, chronic stress, trauma, or illness - contributes to the pathophysiology of various physical and neuropsychiatric disorders. Gene expression is controlled by chromatin plasticity, which involves epigenetic mechanisms such as DNA methylation, histone modifications, and noncoding RNA regulation. Animal models demonstrate that early-life stressors, including maternal deprivation or chronic social stress, induce stable epigenetic changes in key genes, primarily through DNA methylation and histone acetylation. These modifications result in persistent alterations in gene expression, affecting HPA regulation and behavioral phenotypes. Human studies confirm similar epigenetic signatures associated with stress-related learning, memory, and neuropsychiatric conditions. Epigenome-wide association studies have identified stress-related DNA methylation and histone modification profiles linked to both resilience and susceptibility to stress-induced pathologies. Emerging research highlights the role of noncoding RNAs and chromatin remodeling in fine-tuning HPA function, revealing the complex interplay between genetic risk and environmental modulation. These findings suggest promising therapeutic avenues, including pharmacologic agents targeting epigenetic modifiers, psychosocial interventions, and dietary strategies aimed at restoring HPA axis equilibrium to mitigate stress-related disorders.

Handb Clin Neurol · 2026-10-15
Epigenetic mechnisms involved in posttraumatic stress disorder.
Cheishvili D, Hoffman Y, Shrira A
▾

Abstract

Posttraumatic stress disorder (PTSD) is a complex mental health condition influenced by various factors, including the environment, biology, and genetic variations. This chapter delves into how these factors interact, focusing on trauma exposure, brain mechanisms, and genetic expression changes that contribute to the development of PTSD. The chapter provides an overview of PTSD prevalence, diagnostic criteria, key patterns in its occurrence, interindividual symptoms variations, and factors that increase the risk of developing PTSD, such as inherited trauma effects passed down from one generation to the next. It then explores the biologic underpinnings of the disorder, including disruptions in the brain's fear and stress response systems, irregularities in the hypothalamic-pituitary-adrenal axis, and genetic influences that either increase or decrease PTSD risk. Treatment efficacy is also addressed. A significant emphasis is placed on discussing epigenetic mechanisms that may be involved in the molecular pathology of PTSD, mainly focusing on DNA methylation and histone modifications. Research findings from genome-wide studies and research targeting specific stress-related genes, such as the glucocorticoid receptor, its cochaperon FK506 binding protein 5 (FKBP5), or its modulator kinetochore associated complex subunit 2 (SKA2), demonstrate how their epigenetic modulation impacts an individual's stress tolerance. The chapter also covers how trauma-related changes in the body, such as chronic inflammation and immune system irregularities, may be biologically inherited by future generations. Finally, the discussion highlights the potential of epigenetic interventions - targeting the biologic effects of trauma on genes - to pave the way for personalized treatments. Insights from research involving both humans and animals suggest new therapeutic approaches that align with psychologic and biologic perspectives.

Handb Clin Neurol · 2026-10-15
The role of histone modification in epigenetic regulation of gene expression and cell specification.
Di Liegro CM, Schiera G, Di Liegro I
▾

Abstract

During development and differentiation, each cell of multicellular organisms acquires properties fundamental for its specific functions; this happens in the absence of modifications of the DNA nucleotide sequence, and is thus "epigenetic" in nature. The sequential restriction of transcriptional activity to the genes involved in metabolic processes and in cell-specific functions depends on modifications of the structural organization of the DNA-protein complex known as chromatin. Importantly, chromatin structure can change in differentiated cells in response to different stimuli, among which hormonal stimulation, and neurotransmission. The main proteins involved in chromatin structure organization are histones, which can be modified in many ways, and that also exist as isoforms (histone variants), with different structural and functional effects on chromatin. Thanks to sequential epigenetic modifications, cells acquire a memory of their past, stored in their chromatin organization. These processes are even more important in the nervous system, responsible for storing the memory of the entire organism. In this chapter, we will discuss the different types of histone modifications, as well as their effects on chromatin structure and function, with a particular focus on nerve cells as well as on the possible relationships among exercise and diet, and histone modifications.

Hum Reprod Open · 2026-10-14
High sperm DNA fragmentation is associated with increased embryonic chromosomal abnormality: a retrospective study of 8 years involving 5637 couples undergoing ART.
Lou L, Xu P, Xie P, …, Ma S
▾

Abstract

STUDY QUESTION: Does sperm DNA fragmentation impair blastocyst chromosomal integrity? SUMMARY ANSWER: A high sperm DNA fragmentation index (DFI) is associated with an increased risk of blastocyst aneuploidy, particularly when the man has poor semen quality or when the female partner is aged ≥35 years. WHAT IS KNOWN ALREADY: Evidence linking sperm DFI to embryo development and blastocyst chromosomal integrity remains inconsistent, and controversy persists regarding its association with blastocyst aneuploidy detected by preimplantation genetic testing for aneuploidy (PGT-A). Existing studies have often been limited by small sample sizes, inconsistent DFI assays, and insufficient controls of key confounders. STUDY DESIGN SIZE DURATION: This retrospective cohort study was conducted at a tertiary reproductive center. A total of 5637 infertile couples undergoing assisted reproduction between January 2016 and March 2023 were screened, and 3948 couples with complete data were included after applying standardized exclusion criteria. PARTICIPANTS/MATERIALS SETTING METHODS: All male partners who underwent sperm DNA fragmentation assessment using the Sperm Chromatin Structure Assay were included. Infertile couples were stratified by treatment modality, age of both partners, and male semen quality parameters. The primary outcome was blastocyst aneuploidy rate. Beta-binomial regression and zero-inflated beta-binomial regression were used to analyze embryonic outcomes, with model selection guided by the Akaike Information Criterion. Generalized linear mixed models were applied to account for clustering of multiple embryos within the same ovarian stimulation cycle. MAIN RESULTS AND THE ROLE OF CHANCE: Higher DFI was associated with a higher rate of blastocyst aneuploidy. Compared to the low DFI group, the high DFI group had a significantly higher adjusted odds ratio (aOR) of aneuploidy (38.19% vs 24.42%, aOR = 1.35, 95% CI: 1.10-1.81, P = 0.042), while there was no substantial difference between the medium and low DFI group (25.78% vs 24.42%, aOR = 0.93, 95% CI: 0.76-1.13, P = 0.465). Particularly, when women were aged ≥35 years, the embryonic aneuploidy rate in the high DFI group was significantly higher than low DFI subgroup (52.02% vs 38.75%, aOR = 1.61, 95% CI: 1.12-2.32, P = 0.010). Also, among men with severe oligoasthenoteratozoospermia, high DFI significantly increased aneuploidy risk (36.81% vs 18.03%, aOR = 1.99, 95% CI: 1.11-3.57, P = 0.022). For all men aged <40 years, high DFI was associated with a reduced likelihood of high-quality blastocyst formation (aOR = 0.79, 95% CI: 0.64-0.99, P = 0.044). LIMITATIONS REASONS FOR CAUTION: Given the single-center retrospective design, residual confounding cannot be excluded despite rigorous statistical adjustment. WIDER IMPLICATIONS OF THE FINDINGS: Routine sperm DFI assessment may provide clinically meaningful information for infertile couples, particularly those with advanced maternal age or compromised male fertility. These findings support the integration of DFI into embryo risk stratification and individualized treatment planning, potentially improving reproductive outcomes and resource allocation. FUNDING: This work was supported by the National Natural Science Foundation for Young Scientists (72504291), Hunan Provincial Natural Science Foundation of China (2025JJ50703), and the Scientific Research Foundation of Reproductive and Genetic Hospital of CITIC-Xiangya (YNXM-202411). DISCLOSURES: None of the authors have any conflicts of interest to disclose. TRIAL REGISTRATION NUMBER: N/A.

Curr Opin Plant Biol · 2026-10-14
Dynamics and inheritance of the paternal epigenome.
Bourguet P, Jullien PE
▾

Abstract

During plant sexual reproduction, the paternal epigenome is remodelled as it progresses from meiocyte to sperm cell and, after fertilization, to the zygote. In flowering plants, sperm cells retain a condensed chromatin state, specialized histone variants and a relatively stable DNA methylation landscape, whereas the companion vegetative cell undergoes chromatin decondensation, DNA demethylation and transposon reactivation. These contrasting epigenetic states are associated with small RNA production and reinforce genome integrity in the male germline. Following fertilization, sperm-specific histone variants are rapidly removed, and DNA methylation is also reinforced during embryogenesis, a process that may involve mobile maternal small RNAs. We present recent advances in our understanding of the regulation of chromatin states and DNA methylation during male gametogenesis, and discuss how paternal epigenetic information is transmitted, reset or re-established in the early embryo.

Biosystems · 2026-10-13
Biological trajectories toward universal computation: Transposons and chaperone-assisted folding as models of molecular logic.
Sahu A
▾

Abstract

The question of whether biological systems implement computation-like processes is central to synthetic biology and theoretical biophysics. This paper proposes a formal conceptual framework in which two well-characterized molecular mechanisms-transposable elements ("jumping genes") and chaperone-mediated protein folding-are modeled as computational systems. We argue that transposons, exemplified by the ONSEN retrotransposon in Arabidopsis thaliana, implement signal-responsive genome state transitions formally analogous to probabilistic finite-state machine computation, with capacity for computational expressiveness consistent with that of a linear-bounded automaton (LBA) under explicit assumptions of bounded memory, state-dependent branching and heritable epigenetic memory (a hypothesis, not a proof). Simultaneously, molecular chaperones such as heat shock protein 70 (Hsp70) and GroEL/GroES realize an analog computation paradigm, navigating protein energy landscapes through iterative, constraint-driven optimization. Together, these systems suggest that biological macromolecular networks may intrinsically embed logical operations capable of sophisticated information processing. We develop component-level mappings, present a comparative analysis, and outline falsifiable experimental predictions and synthetic biology applications that follow from this framework. These findings carry implications for bio-computing architectures, evolutionary genomics, and the theoretical foundations of life as an information-processing substrate.

J Environ Manage · 2026-10-13
The hologenomic stress legacy: A framework for soil as a transgenerational memory bank of plant immunity.
Nkoh JN, Shang C, Yemele OM, …, Kombe CA
▾

Abstract

Plants pass transgenerational inheritance through genetic and epigenetic information; however, the soil's role as the host environment in this memory transfer remains largely unexplored. Research within this field has largely been domain-specific, with only 117 publications in 24 years integrating fields of soil persistent metabolites (soil chemistry), stress-induced microbial consortia (soil microbiology), and plant molecular science (plant epigenetics). In this perspective article, we synthesize evidence and propose a testable hypothesis, the Hologenomic Stress Legacy (HSL) hypothesis. It states that the soil is an active information-storing center containing biochemical and microbial vectors, persistent metabolites and microbiota, that mediate the transfer of immunity information between plant generations. This hypothesis is supported by a scientometric analysis and critical synthesis of literature related to induced priming, transgenerational inheritance, root exudates, microbial-induced priming, and stress-induced microbiota recruitment (published between January 2000-December 2024). The critical synthesis reveals that abiotically/biotically challenged plants send chemical signals to their roots. This alters root exudation, modifies the rhizosphere, and recruits beneficial microorganisms. Accordingly, the HSL framework proposes that this metabolic and microbial "memory" persists in the soil long after the plant's lifecycle, forming a basis for transgenerational priming. We argue that resilience is not just encoded in the plant's genome; it extends to the host environment (the soil) and could potentially be deciphered or engineered for agricultural benefit. Finally, we propose an experimental roadmap to validate this hypothesis, which includes a computational chemistry component (Computational Chemistry Framework for HSL-CCF4H) to help optimize the transfer of this "soil memory" and begin moving from correlational observations to mechanistic causality.

Zhu C, Pan W, Zheng J, …, Zhang H
▾

Abstract

Transfer RNA-derived small RNAs (tsRNAs) are emerging epigenetic regulators involved in environmental adaptation and paternal effects. Their roles in fish offspring alkaline tolerance, however, remain unclear. In this study, small non-coding RNA (sncRNA) profiles of grass carp (Ctenopharyngodon idella) sperm, oocytes, and embryos were characterized by high-throughput sequencing, and the potential functions of sperm-associated tsRNAs were further investigated. Predicted target genes were significantly enriched in pathways related to stress response, ion homeostasis, epithelial remodeling, and energy metabolism. Functional microinjection assays in zebrafish and grass carp embryos showed that tRF-33-R9JP9PMNH5SYDQ and tRF-33-KQI34JRX6N380Q significantly enhanced offspring survival under alkaline stress, whereas inhibition of these tsRNAs reduced alkaline tolerance. These findings suggest that sperm-derived tsRNAs may participate in regulating offspring alkaline tolerance in fish and may provide candidate molecular targets for future alkaline-tolerant breeding programs.

Ageing Res Rev · 2026-10-13
Exercise, DNA methylation, and Alzheimer's disease: Mechanistic links and future directions in intergenerational research.
Said G, Ramos da Silva AS, da Silva Rodrigues G
▾

Abstract

Alzheimer's disease (AD) is the most common cause of dementia and remains without a cure; recently introduced disease-modifying therapies provide only modest slowing in selected patients. Physical exercise is associated with better cognitive and functional outcomes, but the molecular mechanisms underlying these benefits remain incompletely understood. DNA methylation is a potential interface linking environmental stimuli to gene regulation and neural function. This narrative review examines evidence connecting exercise, DNA methylation, and AD, with emphasis on AD-related loci and on the possible intergenerational implications of parental exercise. We examine methylation findings involving amyloid precursor protein (APP) and presenilin-1 (PSEN1), which are relevant to amyloid-β production; microtubule-associated protein tau (MAPT), which encodes tau; and apolipoprotein E (APOE), which influences lipid transport and amyloid-β clearance. These findings suggest potential epigenetic contributions to AD-related biology, although their direction and functional significance vary across studies. We also discuss exercise-responsive pathways involved in energy sensing, autophagy, and redox signaling. We also evaluate whether exercise-associated epigenetic remodeling could influence amyloid-β accumulation, Tau homeostasis, and neuronal function. Evidence that exercise modifies DNA methylation is growing, whereas studies that integrate exercise interventions, genome-wide methylation, and AD-related outcomes remain scarce. Evidence for an inherited reduction in AD susceptibility is even more limited and currently supports a hypothesis rather than an established mechanism. Future studies integrating behavioral, neuropathological, cell-type-resolved epigenomic, and other multi-omic outcomes are needed to determine whether exercise-induced DNA methylation changes mediate neuroprotection in AD or primarily reflect broader physiological adaptation.

Front Urol · 2026-10-13
Randomized non-inferiority within-patient trial of density gradient processing of sequential ejaculates versus centrifugation-free sperm separation device for ICSI in men with elevated sperm DNA fragmentation- study protocol.
Chandra V, Sanagoudar S, Shinde J, …, Esteves SC
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: Pathological sperm DNA fragmentation (SDF) has been associated with impaired fertilization, compromised embryo development, recurrent pregnancy loss, and lower pregnancy rates in assisted reproductive technology (ART). Two same-day strategies proposed to reduce SDF prior to in vitro fertilization/intracytoplasmic sperm injection (IVF/ICSI) are: (i) processing a second ejaculate obtained after a short abstinence interval using density gradient centrifugation (DGC), and (ii) selecting sperm from the first ejaculate using a centrifugation-free sperm separation device (CFSS). Although both approaches may enrich sperm populations with lower DNA damage, direct comparative evidence regarding reproductive outcomes remains limited. METHODS AND ANALYSIS: This prospective, multicenter, within-patient, sibling-oocyte randomized non-inferiority trial will be conducted at nine tertiary ART centers in India. Couples undergoing ICSI will be eligible if the male partner is aged 21-45 years with elevated SDF (DNA fragmentation index >20% by sperm chromatin dispersion assay within 90 days before enrollment) and the female partner is aged 21-35 years and expected to yield at least four metaphase II oocytes. On the day of oocyte retrieval, the first ejaculate collected after 2-3 days of abstinence will undergo processing using the LensHooke® CA0 centrifugation-free sperm separation device, whereas a second ejaculate obtained 1-3 hours later will be processed using conventional DGC. Sibling metaphase II oocytes will be randomized 1:1 to ICSI using sperm from either intervention arm. Embryos will be cultured to the blastocyst stage and graded according to standardized criteria. If both arms generate at least one transferable blastocyst (≥3BB), the embryo selected for the first transfer will be randomized according to its arm of origin; otherwise, transfer will proceed using the available embryo(s) according to a predefined rescue strategy. The primary outcome is ongoing pregnancy rate at 12 weeks' gestation following transfer of an embryo originating from the randomized study arm. Secondary outcomes include post-processing SDF and sperm parameters, normal fertilization, blastocyst formation and quality, implantation, biochemical and clinical pregnancy. Non-inferiority of DGC processing of the second ejaculate compared with centrifugation-free sperm selection will be assessed using a predefined absolute non-inferiority margin of 10 percentage points. ETHICS AND DISSEMINATION: The study protocol has been approved by the institutional review boards of all participating centers. Written informed consent will be obtained from all participants. Study findings will be disseminated through peer-reviewed publications and scientific meetings. CLINICAL TRIAL REGISTRATION: https://clinicaltrials.gov/, identifier NCT07626359.

Annu Rev Pharmacol Toxicol · 2026-10-13
Environmental Epigenetic Transgenerational Inheritance and Generational Toxicology.
Bahrainian Y, Nilsson EE, Skinner MK
▾

Abstract

Environmental chemicals and toxicants can promote phenotypic variation or disease not only in the individual exposed but also in individuals for multiple subsequent generations. This phenomenon is termed epigenetic transgenerational inheritance and generational toxicology. Most environmental factors and toxicants do not promote genetic mutations that alter the DNA sequence, but they can alter epigenetics and the epigenome. Epigenetic changes in the germline allow for epigenetic transgenerational inheritance and generational toxicology as a result of the previous generations' exposures. The toxicology and pharmacology of environmental chemicals and epigenetic transgenerational inheritance are discussed in the context of generational toxicology.

Abstract

BACKGROUND: As delayed parenthood becomes a global demographic norm, understanding the biological consequences of advanced parental age or APrA (≥35 years in mothers; ≥40 years in fathers) is a critical geroscience priority. Ultimately, this reframes aging as a continuous intergenerational process rather than an isolated individual trajectory. GENERAL SIGNIFICANCE: Bridging reproductive biology and geroscience, this framework decodes how parental age shapes offspring biological legacy, essential for refining reproductive counseling and informing public health strategies.

▾

Abstract

The ovary ages earlier than most other organs, but the molecular mechanisms that drive this decline remain unclear. To gain more insight into this process, we profiled ovaries from young and reproductively aged mice after synchronized ovulation and found that aging was marked by the accumulation of luteal cells with increased steroidogenic, antigen presentation and inflammatory gene expression. DNA methylation was globally preserved in aged ovaries but showed focal age-associated changes at gene regulatory regions. PLoS Biol · 2026-10-09
FuChi is a cell cycle biosensor for tracking cell cycle dynamics during avian development.
Sudderick ZR, Briggs T, Mubarak S, …, Glover JD
▾

Abstract

The ability to monitor the proliferative status of live cells both in vitro and in vivo over time has revolutionised our understanding of development, growth and disease. This was first made possible by fluorescent ubiquitination-based cell cycle indicator (Fucci) technology, which distinguishes specific cell cycle phases through the reciprocal degradation of fluorescently tagged, truncated forms of human CDT1 and GMNN proteins. Fucci genetic systems have been successfully implemented in transgenic mice, zebrafish, and axolotls. To date, no viable, stably expressing Fucci line has been developed in an avian species. Although a range of continuously improving Fucci constructs have been developed in recent years, existing in vivo Fucci models remain limited because they rely on older reporter technology that fails to distinguish cells in S, G2, and M phases or to label cells in early G1. As a result, these models can be challenging to interpret and their utility for continuous cell tracking and precise analysis of cell-cycle dynamics is limited. Here, we introduce FuChi, a multicistronic Fucci-expressing chicken line incorporating a newly optimised reporter construct composed of an mCerulean-tagged histone H1.0 linker protein fused via a self-cleaving 2A peptide to the tandem Fucci(CA)2 cell cycle biosensor, with additional epitope tags included for detection in fixed tissues. We show that this system accurately discriminates and permits tracking of cells in G1, S, G2, and M phases both in vitro and in vivo, enabling faithful visualisation of cell cycle status in intact tissues and organs. Using FuChi embryos, we mapped proliferation dynamics across developing tissues, analysed cell cycle states of migrating cells, and performed live imaging of early embryos. These latter experiments revealed that transition from S phase may be a key morphogenetic event during gastrulation as mesendoderm cells egress from the primitive streak to form embryonic structures including the prechordal plate. Pairing this advanced reporter with the intrinsic experimental advantages of the chicken embryo positions FuChi as a premier in vivo system for studying cell-cycle kinetics in development, delivering clear technological improvements over current Fucci models. FuChi chickens provide a powerful new resource for studying embryonic development, organ growth, tissue homeostasis, disease processes, and infection responses.

Acta Med Indones · 2026-10-08
Metabolic Imprinting and Intergenerational Cardiometabolic Risk in Human Populations: Implications for Preconception and Maternal Health.
Herman H, Takaryanto D
▾

Abstract

The global rise in obesity and type 2 diabetes has been too rapid to be explained by genetic change alone, which points to developmental and environmental contributions to cardiometabolic risk. Metabolic conditions during sensitive windows, particularly preconception, pregnancy, and early postnatal life, appear to shape long-term metabolic regulation and disease susceptibility. In this narrative review, we synthesise human cohort studies, natural experiments, and experimental models, and we translate the resulting evidence into clinical and public health practice. Across human cohorts, maternal metabolic disturbances such as gestational diabetes and obesity are consistently associated with higher offspring risk of obesity, insulin resistance, and impaired glucose regulation, with adjusted estimates commonly between 1.4- and 2.0-fold. Epigenome-wide studies report differential DNA methylation in insulin-signalling, adipogenic, and energy-homeostasis pathways, lending biological plausibility, and experimental models show that transient early perturbations can durably alter metabolic physiology even after diet is later normalised. We read these converging findings as metabolic imprinting: relatively stable but potentially modifiable regulatory states, rather than deterministic germline inheritance. Set against current guidance from the ADA, FIGO, WHO, USPSTF, and ACOG, the evidence supports concrete actions across the reproductive life course, including preconception screening and weight optimisation, gestational diabetes screening and glycaemic management, appropriate gestational weight gain, and postpartum and offspring follow-up. Preconception and maternal metabolic health therefore emerge as practical, high-value targets for reducing the long-term cardiometabolic burden.

Histone acetyltransferase KAT7 is associated with mitochondrial function during porcine oocyte maturation.
Huang T, Kader A, Chen Z, …, Zhang Y
▾

Abstract

The MYST family of lysine acetyltransferase (KAT) are required for many critical nuclear functions, including transcription and DNA damage repair in somatic cells; however, the functions of individual MYST members in mammalian oocyte meiosis remain largely unclear. Here, we show that KAT7, as a key MYST family member, might support mitochondrial function during porcine oocyte maturation. We found that KAT7 localized to the cytoplasm during meiotic progression. Protoplasma · 2026-10-07
Methylated memories mechanism, evidence, and skepticism in plant transgenerational epigenetic inheritance.
Singh G
▾

Abstract

Plants are repeatedly offered as the strongest case that organisms can inherit acquired experience without a change in DNA sequence - a claim that brushes against more than a century of Weismannian orthodoxy. DNA methylation, small-RNA pathways, and chromatin marks can be reshaped by heat, drought, salinity, and pathogen attack, and in several plant systems these marks persist into unstressed offspring. Yet the field remains split. Well-controlled studies find epigenetic memory to be shallow, genotype-dependent, and easily confused with parental provisioning or incomplete resetting during gametogenesis, while a smaller set of striking systems - among them paramutation at the maize b1 locus and stress-induced methylation in clonal dandelions - show that heritable, sequence-independent memory is mechanistically real, at least in specific genomic contexts. This review focuses on DNA methylation, the best-characterized carrier of heritable epigenetic information in plants, and in particular on RNA-directed DNA methylation (RdDM), the de novo pathway most closely linked to stress, while placing it alongside the MET1 and chromomethylase pathways and chromatin-based memory. It uses maize paramutation and dandelion stress memory as two contrasting, illustrative case studies, situates them within the wider body of stress-methylation experiments in Arabidopsis, rice, and other species, lays out the skeptical counter-case, and considers why commercial interest in "climate-smart" epigenetically primed seed is forcing the field to resolve these questions faster than the data currently allow.

Obesity (Silver Spring) · 2026-10-07
tsRNAs Mediate Intergenerational Inheritance by Concertedly Suppressing Wnt and PPAR Pathways.
Zhou J, Liu L, Zhang Y, …, Zeng L
▾

Abstract

OBJECTIVE: The Developmental Origins of Health and Disease hypothesis posits that paternal environmental exposures program offspring metabolic health via sperm epigenetic factors, particularly tRNA-derived small RNAs (tsRNAs). Cadmium (Cd) is a ubiquitous environmental pollutant known to impair reproductive health, but epigenetic links to offspring metabolic disorders are unclear. Using paternal Cd exposure, we elucidate sperm tsRNA pathogenic mechanisms in intergenerational inheritance. METHODS: We validated candidate tsRNA functions via zygotic microinjection of agomirs in mice and transfection in AML12 cells in vitro. For mechanistic insights, we conducted blastocyst low-input RNA sequencing, liver transcriptomics, and in vitro assays (luciferase, Western blot, immunofluorescence). RESULTS: Transfection of Cd-upregulated sperm tsRNAs (tRF-Leu-CAA, tRF-Ala-AGC) inhibited PPAR signaling in AML12 cells and caused lipid disorders. In vivo, microinjection recapitulated paternal Cd effects: impaired embryogenesis and adult metabolic syndromes (weight gain, hyperlipidemia, hepatic steatosis). Mechanistically, tsRNAs suppressed Wnt and PPAR pathways from the two-cell stage, with LEF1/WNT5A as direct Wnt targets; this suppression persisted in adult liver. CONCLUSIONS: We propose a two-hit mechanism: first hit via early embryonic Wnt/PPAR co-suppression, second hit via sustained inhibition into adulthood, driving metabolic dysregulation. Thus, tsRNAs are key epigenetic vectors linking paternal Cd exposure to offspring metabolic health.

Front Epigenet Epigenom · 2026-10-07
Mobile small RNAs as mediators of stress-induced epigenomic remodeling and systemic stress adaptation.
Junaid MD, Iqbal MS, Nasir MW, Akhter MW
▾

Abstract

Plants rely on sophisticated long-distance communication networks to coordinate adaptive responses to changing environmental conditions. Among these, mobile small RNAs (sRNAs), including microRNAs (miRNAs) and small interfering RNAs (siRNAs), function as critical systemic signaling molecules that move between tissues through plasmodesmata and the phloem. While their roles in post-transcriptional gene regulation are well established, emerging evidence indicates that mobile sRNAs also mediate stress-induced epigenomic remodeling in recipient tissues. Specifically, 24-nt siRNAs can direct RNA-dependent DNA methylation (RdDM) and chromatin modifications at distal genomic loci, thereby altering transcriptional states, stabilizing stress-responsive gene networks, and modulating transposon activity. Such epigenetic reprogramming can extend beyond transient stress responses, contributing to stress memory and, in some cases, to transgenerational inheritance of adaptive traits. This review synthesizes current knowledge on the biogenesis, transport, and functional integration of mobile sRNAs, with special emphasis on their capacity to reshape DNA methylation landscapes and chromatin architecture during environmental stress. We further discuss experimental evidence from grafting studies, methylome profiling, and mutant analyses that support systemic epigenetic regulation mediated by sRNAs. Finally, we explore the practical applications of leveraging mobile sRNA-driven epigenomic remodeling for sustainable crop improvement, including epi-grafting and targeted epigenome engineering strategies. By positioning mobile sRNAs as mediators of systemic epigenetic plasticity, this review emphasizes their central role in coordinating plant stress adaptation in a changing climate.

J Anim Sci · 2026-10-06
RGFP966 Exhibits an LH-Like Efficacy in Promoting Tan Sheep Oocyte Maturation and Subsequent Developmental Competence.
Wang X, Li P, Lv D, …, Liu X
▾

Abstract

RGFP966 is a selective inhibitor of histone deacetylase 3 (HDAC3). This study aimed to evaluate whether HDAC3 inhibition could promote in vitro maturation (IVM) and subsequent embryo development in Tan sheep oocytes, and to compare its efficacy with luteinizing hormone (LH). Immunofluorescence analysis revealed that HDAC3 expression was significantly decreased in metaphase II (MII) oocytes compared to the germinal vesicle (GV) stage (P < 0.001). Western blotting verified that RGFP966 suppressed HDAC3 deacetylase activity in Tan sheep granulosa cells, increasing the acetylation of downstream substrates H3K9ac, H3K14ac and H4K5ac (P < 0.01). Treatment with RGFP966 significantly promoted polar body 1 (PB1) extrusion, achieving a maturation rate comparable to the LH-treated group. Mechanistically, RGFP966 treatment significantly enhanced mitochondrial distribution (P < 0.01), upregulated GPX4 expression (P < 0.001), and accordingly reduced intracellular reactive oxygen species (ROS) levels (P < 0.001). Moreover, RGFP966 significantly decreased the rate of spindle/chromosome misalignment (P < 0.001) and reduced early apoptosis (indicated by reduced Annexin-V staining) in MII oocytes (P < 0.001), without inducing additional DNA double strand breaks, as evidenced by unchanged γH2AX levels. Subsequent in vitro fertilization (IVF) demonstrated that the 2-cell cleavage and blastocyst rates of oocytes matured with RGFP966 were comparable to those of the LH group, and the resultant blastocysts exhibited normal expression patterns of lineage markers (CDX2 and SOX2). In conclusion, HDAC3 inhibitor RGFP966 promotes Tan sheep oocyte IVM and subsequent developmental competence with an efficacy equivalent to LH. This process relies on alleviating oxidative stress by enhancing mitochondrial distribution and GPX4 expression, while preserving genomic stability during meiosis. These findings provide a theoretical basis for the potential application of HDAC3 inhibitors as effective additives in ovine IVM systems.

Abstract

Early follicular development and primordial follicle formation are essential for ovarian reserve establishment, and disruption of this process may contribute to fertility disorders such as premature ovarian insufficiency (POI). Theriogenology · 2026-10-06
Embryo-derived extracellular vesicle miRNA cargo reflects developmental competence in porcine in vitro-produced embryos.
Gascon-Collado D, Blanco-Cueto P, Cabrera-Gómez CG, …, Martinez-Serrano CA
▾

Abstract

Extracellular vesicles (EVs) have emerged as important mediators of intercellular communication during embryo development and represent a promising source of non-invasive biomarkers of embryo developmental competence. This study characterized for the first time EVs released by porcine in vitro-produced embryos and compared the microRNA (miRNA) cargo of EVs derived from blastocysts (BL) and non-blastocysts (N-BL) to identify molecular signatures associated with developmental competence. Porcine embryos were produced by standard in vitro maturation, fertilization, and culture procedures. On day 7 of in vitro culture, embryos were classified as BL and N-BL, washed and cultured in groups of 50 for 6 h. Conditioned media were subjected to EV isolation by size-exclusion chromatography and characterized by dynamic light scattering, transmission electron microscopy, and flow cytometry. Total RNA was extracted and subjected to small RNA sequencing. No significant differences were detected in EV concentration or mean particle size between groups. A total of 123 miRNAs were identified, including 105 shared, 15 BL-exclusive, and 3 N-BL-exclusive. Among the shared miRNAs, ssc-miR-148a, ssc-miR-99a, ssc-miR-122, and ssc-miR-151a were upregulated, whereas ssc-miR-181b was downregulated in BL-derived EVs. Functional enrichment analysis revealed biological processes associated with developmental signaling, cellular organization and vesicle trafficking in BL-derived EVs, whereas N-BL-derived EVs were associated with apoptosis, cytokine-mediated signaling, developmental arrest and epigenetic regulation. Collectively, these findings demonstrate that the miRNA cargo of embryo-derived EVs reflects embryo developmental competence and identify EV-associated miRNAs as promising non-invasive biomarkers and candidate regulators of early embryonic development in porcine in vitro production systems.

Abstract

CONTEXT: The efficiency of in vitro embryo production in animal species is constrained by the absence of practical, non-invasive biomarkers to assess oocyte quality. AIMS: This study investigated the association of CC-DFI with in vitro maturation (IVM) and intracytoplasmic sperm injection (ICSI) outcomes in horses, and its relationship with mare-related factors, autumnal transition period, cumulus cell viability, and cumulus expansion. CONCLUSIONS: In conclusion, this study supports CC-DFI as a potential biomarker for predicting oocyte developmental potential in equine ICSI, particularly blastocyst formation. Science · IF 45.8 · 2026-10-08
Epigenetic aging and transposon dysregulation reflect size-related lifespan compression in dogs.
Mariner BL, McCoy BM, Greenier A, …, Tolbert MK
▾

Abstract

The extraordinary lifespan variation in domestic dogs provides a natural experiment for testing how intrinsic rates of biological aging shape lifespan. Using 1640 methylomes from 894 dogs, we developed an epigenetic clock that predicted mortality and demonstrated that epigenetic aging is fastest early in life. At orthologs of human age-associated genes, dogs exhibited concordant age effects on promoter methylation, highlighting conserved remodeling of immune pathways. We found that larger and male dogs, which are shorter lived, exhibit accelerated molecular aging. Distinct epigenetic architectures mediated these effects: Sex-dependent methylation changes were concentrated on the X chromosome, whereas size-associated methylation was especially pronounced at transposable elements (TEs). These findings show that epigenetics reflects lifespan differences in dogs and identifies TEs as potential mediators of size-associated lifespan.

Somatic epimutations cap genetic determinism in the human diploid chromatin epigenome.
Vollger MR, Swanson EG, Neph SJ, …, Stergachis AB
▾

Abstract

Diploid human cells contain two non-identical genomes, and differences in their regulation underlie human development and disease. We present Fiber-seq inferred regulatory elements (FIRE) and show that FIRE provides a more comprehensive and quantitative snapshot of the accessible chromatin landscape across the 6 Gbp diploid human genome, overcoming previously unrecognized biases in existing regulatory element catalogs. FIRE enables comprehensive detection of haplotype-selective chromatin accessibility (HSCA), exposing imprinted elements lacking underlying parent-of-origin differences in DNA methylation and gene regulatory modules that permit genes to escape X chromosome inactivation. We uncover that the human leukocyte antigen (HLA) locus harbors the most HSCA in immune cells, where we resolve specific transcription factor (TF) binding events disrupted by disease-associated variants. Finally, we demonstrate that the regulatory landscape of a cell is littered with autosomal somatic chromatin epimutations that are propagated by clonal expansions to create mitotically stable and non-genetically deterministic chromatin alterations.

A telomere-to-telomere map of somatic mutation burden and functional impact in a cancer.

▾

Abstract

Cancer genomes harbor extensive somatic genetic and epigenetic variation, yet large portions of the genome remain inaccessible to conventional reference-based analyses. Here, we demonstrate that pairing a near-telomere-to-telomere (near-T2T) diploid assembly of a donor with deep short- and long-read sequencing of their melanoma reveals that 16% of somatic variants localize to sequences absent from GRCh38, with satellite repeats acting as hotspots for UV-associated mutagenesis. Abstract

Exercise reduces all-cause mortality, yet its molecular effects across organs, sexes, and circadian phases remain incompletely understood. Nat Rev Genet · IF 39.1 · 2026-10-06
Decoding epithelial-mesenchymal transitions with multi-omics.
Narwade N, Nieto MA
▾

Abstract

Epithelial-mesenchymal transition (EMT) is a highly dynamic and plastic cellular programme essential for embryonic development, tissue repair and ageing, and it is co-opted in pathological conditions such as cancer and fibrosis. The continuum of intermediate states and pronounced cellular heterogeneity that characterize EMT have historically limited systematic investigation across regulatory layers, including the transcriptome, epigenome, proteome and metabolome. Recent advances in high-throughput omics technologies, particularly single-cell and spatially resolved platforms, have enabled comprehensive profiling of cellular states at unprecedented resolution. These approaches, in parallel with computational methodologies such as machine learning frameworks, network-based modelling and integrative multi-omics, have been instrumental in extracting biological insight from intrinsically complex datasets. In this Review, we discuss how the convergence of emerging omics technologies and computational frameworks has advanced EMT research, outline key analytical considerations and discuss current challenges and future opportunities for biomarker discovery and translational applications.

Nat Methods · IF 36.1 · 2026-10-30
Enhanced cfDNA methylation profiling by detection and correction of jagged ends.
Wang R, Hamilton EG, Almanza D, …, Alizadeh AA
▾

Abstract

Analysis of cell-free DNA (cfDNA) methylation can enable noninvasive disease detection by leveraging tissue-specific epigenetic differences between malignant and nonmalignant cells. However, such analyses can be confounded by the repair of jagged DNA fragment ends during double-stranded sequencing library preparation, which introduces spurious methylation signals into cfDNA molecules. Here we present JEEPERS, a framework to detect and correct jagged-end-induced methylation errors while preserving duplex integrity and native methylation patterns. We showed high fidelity for measuring tumor burden and demonstrated allele-specific methylation differences at recurrent somatic mutations, revealing a link between adjacent CpG methylation and EGFR/TP53 driver and resistance mutations. We further demonstrated enhanced diagnostic performance across liquid biopsy applications, including tumor-type-specific methylation profiles, hemimethylation profiling, epigenetic-clock-based age estimation and cell type deconvolution. By faithfully correcting end-repair errors, JEEPERS provides a versatile framework for integrating mutations and methylation features in cfDNA at high resolution, enabling more precise molecular profiling and disease monitoring.

DCAF7 regulates hematopoietic stem cell function and differentiation through polycomb repressive complex 1 modulation.
Melo-Cardenas J, Bezavada L, Ling T, …, Crispino JD
▾

Abstract

DCAF7 is one of the few DCAFs expressed in hematopoietic stem cells (HSCs). Using a conditional knockout mouse model, we discovered that the absence of DCAF7 leads to accumulation of differentiating progenitors in the bone marrow, and impairs self-renewal capacity of HSCs upon transplantation. Together, these data reveal that DCAF7 is a novel regulator of the PRC1 complex and epigenetic enzymes that control HSC differentiation.

Nat Commun · IF 14.7 · 2026-11-02
A multi-center, single-arm, phase 2 trial of dasatinib in T follicular helper cell lymphomas.
Suma S, Sakata-Yanagimoto M, Suehara Y, …, Chiba S
▾

Abstract

T follicular helper cell lymphomas (TFHL) harbor activating T cell receptor (TCR) signaling mutations and epigenetic alterations, such as TET2 mutations. Here, we conduct a multi-center, single-arm, phase 2 trial of the tyrosine kinase inhibitor dasatinib in patients with relapsed/refractory TFHLs (jRCT2031190079). The primary outcome is the overall response rate (ORR) according to CT-based Lugano criteria 2014. Key secondary endpoints include ORR in RHOA-mutated patients and progression-free/overall survival. The main inclusion criteria consist of TFHL diagnosis and relapse following at least 1 prior chemotherapy regimen. Key exclusion criteria encompass central nervous system involvement, history of other malignancies, and receipt of chemotherapy or radiotherapy within 4 weeks. Nineteen patients are enrolled, with 18 receiving dasatinib and 17 evaluable for efficacy. Median age is 73 years, 50% are female, and angioimmunoblastic T cell lymphoma is the predominant subtype (n = 13). Dasatinib yields a 23.5% ORR, below the prespecified 30% endpoint. Treatment-related adverse events occur in 61.1% of patients (n = 11). Patients harboring TCR signaling mutations, especially those with RHOA p.G17V, achieve a 36.4% ORR, whereas no responses are observed in mutation-negative cases. Exploratory single-cell RNA sequencing reveals interferon-γ/tumor necrosis factor-enriched tumor microenvironments and peripheral expansion of cytotoxic T cell clones in responders. Conversely, early progression correlates with a high TET2 mutation burden in non-malignant immune cells, including CD8+ T cell subsets. While the primary outcome is below the prespecified endpoint, our exploratory findings implicate immune dysfunction involving TET2 mutations in limiting the therapeutic benefit of dasatinib.

Nat Commun · IF 14.7 · 2026-10-30
RuPHOTACs provide photocontrol over protein degradation with optimized properties for biological applications.
Havrylyuk D, LaMore AP, Al-Hamaly MA, …, Glazer EC
▾

Abstract

PROteolysis TArgeting Chimeras (PROTACs) are bifunctional molecules that catalyze degradation of selected proteins by inducing protein:protein interactions (PPIs) between E3 ubiquitin ligases and the protein of interest. A critical limitation is undesired effects in untargeted tissues, necessitating approaches to impose spatiotemporal control over PROTAC function. Here we present Ru(II) photocages that can be released with low energy light, providing triggered PROTAC activity on demand. The systems, termed Ruthenium-based PHOToActivated Chimeras (RuPHOTACs), were validated by targeting bromodomain-containing proteins, which act as crucial epigenetic regulators, and also strongly reduced levels of c-MYC and PIM1. RuPHOTACs demonstrate that the incorporation of metal components within organic PROTACs confers multiple advantages for light-controlled systems for chemical biology applications, including the highest selectivity for activation in the light vs. the dark, improved potency against the target proteins of interest, and increased efficacy in vivo using low energy red light. To efficiently monitor protein degradation via optical means, a strategy was implemented by creating a fusion of a photoconvertible protein, Dendra2, with the proteins of interest. This bifunctional reporter system for live cell analysis decouples protein degradation efficiency and rates from signals arising from new protein production, and is superior to prior reporter systems described for PROTACs because it directly measures the true degradation rate of the target protein in intact, viable cells while simultaneously tracking newly synthesized protein without perturbing translation, proteostasis, or cell health.

A SAGA-like AP2-P-ARP1-GCN5 transcriptional complex targets pathogenesis-associated genes during the blood-stage development of Babesia gibsoni.
Han Z, Zheng Y, Liu W, …, Zhao J
▾

Abstract

Babesiosis is an increasingly recognized tick-borne zoonosis. The asexual blood stage of its causative agent, Babesia, drives pathological damages in the host and can cause severe or fatal human infections. Nevertheless, the transcriptional regulatory mechanisms that control this developmental stage remain poorly characterized. Here, we identified a novel ApiAP2 transcription factor (AP2-P; pathogenesis), which is essential in the blood-stage development of B. gibsoni. Nuclear import of AP2-P is inhibited by ivermectin, which is likely mediated via the IMPα/β pathway. Importantly, AP2-P forms a SAGA-like complex with the histone acetyltransferase GCN5 and a novel AP2-P related protein 1 (ARP1). Notably, normal genomic distribution pattern of AP2-P likewise depends on ARP1 or GCN5. Collectively, our study suggests a SAGA-like epigenetic module responsible for pathogenesis of Babesia gibsoni during asexual blood stage.

Inflammatory memory primes the intestine for enhanced regenerative potential.
Arozamena B, Moraitis I, Mularoni L, …, Guiu J

Abstract

How do inflammation and activation of the regenerative genetic program affect intestinal epithelial cell behavior and shape responses to future injuries? Nat Commun · IF 14.7 · 2026-10-27
An epigenetic aging-informed dietary pattern is associated with a spectrum of aging-related health outcomes.
Lai S, Yu J, Zhang L, …, Chen H
▾

Abstract

Diet is associated with biological aging, yet few existing dietary indices have been developed specifically to target biological aging. We develop a data-driven, food-based Empirical Dietary Index for Slower Epigenetic Aging (EDISEA) to identify dietary features predictive of lower GrimAge2 acceleration. Outcome-wide analyses in a large UK population-based cohort further show broad health associations, supported by molecular and neuroimaging signatures of more favourable vascular, inflammatory, metabolic, and brain structural profiles. EDISEA may provide an epigenetic aging-informed framework for studying dietary strategies relevant to healthy aging.

Abstract

▾

Abstract

Nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide (NAD) is a metabolic cofactor regulating energy metabolism, epigenetics, DNA repair, and signaling. Although NAD declines with vitamin B3 deficiency, disease, and aging, systemic consequences of chronic depletion remain unclear. Here, we characterize an in vivo model of inducible NAD deficiency using acquired niacin dependency (ANDY) mice, in which NAD synthesis becomes dependent on dietary niacin. Senolytic treatment partially improved functional decline without restoring NAD. Abstract

Lung maturation involves intricate cellular differentiation and structural remodeling, yet the underlying epigenetic dynamics remain poorly characterized at single-cell resolution. Nat Commun · IF 14.7 · 2026-10-21
The DNMT1 CXXC domain senses CpG islands for DNA methylation inhibition.
Fang J, Pan B, Ren W, …, Song J
▾

Abstract

DNA methyltransferase DNMT1 plays an essential role in maintaining DNA methylation homeostasis. While DNMT1-mediated DNA methylation mainly occurs outside the active chromatin domains, such as CpG islands, how DNMT1 interplays with hypomethylated nucleosome environment to restrict CpG island methylation remains unclear. Here we report the structure of DNMT1 with a nucleosome containing unmodified CpG sites. The interaction of the DNMT1 CXXC domain with the unmodified CpG sites within the nucleosome core or the linker strengthens the DNMT1-nucleosome association, which contributes to the enrichment of DNMT1 in CpG islands. On the other hand, the CXXC domain is positioned in a manner that blocks the target recognition domain of DNMT1 from accessing potential DNA substrates. Consistently, disrupting the CXXC-CpG interaction leads to DNMT1 hyperactivity on nucleosomes with unmodified CpG sites, or those with spatially proximate unmodified and hemimethylated CpG sites. Furthermore, our whole-genome bisulfite-sequencing analysis reveals that introducing the CXXC-CpG interaction-defective DNMT1 mutation leads to substantial increase in CpG island methylation but not so much in overall genomic methylation, causing severe defects in transcriptional programming and stem cell differentiation. Together, this study establishes a role for the CXXC domain in safeguarding the CpG island hypomethylation, vital to DNA methylation homeostasis during development.

Nat Commun · IF 14.7 · 2026-10-21
BEAR-GRN: Systematic assessment of single-cell multi-omics-based gene regulatory network inference methods.
Karamveer K, Moeller E, Valensi H, …, Uzun Y
▾

Abstract

Identifying regulatory gene interactions is inherently challenging as it depends on both the transcriptional and epigenetic landscapes. Multiple methods are proposed to infer gene regulatory networks (GRNs) using single-cell multi-omics data, yet the absence of a standardized benchmarking framework remains an obstacle to unbiased performance evaluation. Here we present BEAR-GRN, a systematic resource providing standardized datasets, curated ground-truth networks, and a unified computational pipeline for benchmarking GRN inference methods. Using BEAR-GRN, we assess state-of-the-art multi-omics GRN inference methods across paired single-cell RNA-seq and ATAC-seq datasets spanning human and mouse cell types. We evaluate accuracy against four complementary ground-truth definitions, alongside computational efficiency, output stability, and modality contribution. Although ground-truth choice profoundly influences performance, LINGER and DIRECT-NET show the highest overall accuracy and stability. Modality perturbation experiments reveal that current methods are predominantly RNA-driven, with chromatin accessibility contributing limited independent signal. BEAR-GRN enables researchers to evaluate existing and new GRN methods against established baselines.

Nat Commun · IF 14.7 · 2026-10-20
Mammalian DNA maintenance methylation depends on histone but not PAF15 ubiquitination.
Li J, Huang Y, Sun M, …, Wong J
▾

Abstract

In mammals, DNA methylation is an essential inheritable epigenetic modification maintained by the DNMT1-UHRF1 machinery in a UHRF1 E3 ubiquitin ligase-activity-dependent manner. UHRF1-catalyzed histone ubiquitination and PAF15 ubiquitination have been proposed to recruit DNMT1 to hemi-methylated CpGs. Here, we confirm that both PAF15 and histone H3 are robustly ubiquitinated by UHRF1 in a replication-dependent manner during S phase. However, loss of PAF15 or its ubiquitination, or overexpression of ubiquitination-deficient PAF15, does not alter DNMT1 recruitment to replicating DNA or global DNA methylation. We show that UHRF1-mediated PAF15 ubiquitination requires DNMT1, is strictly replication-coupled, and that PAF15 and histone ubiquitination are mediated by distinct UHRF1 pools. Taken together, our study demonstrates that hemi-methylated-DNA-stimulated local histone ubiquitination, rather than replication-coupled PAF15 ubiquitination, drives DNMT1 targeting for maintenance methylation.

Long J, Yang C, Xu S, …, Feng X
▾

Abstract

Small RNAs (sRNAs) are widespread across diverse flowering plant species and play important roles regulating reproduction and environmental responses. However, how sRNAs exert their function is largely unknown, likely due to their typical origin in non-genic regions, the lack of distinct gene targets, and the sparse characterization of their associated Argonaute (AGO) effector proteins. These molecular disruptions are accompanied by significant meiotic defects. Together, our findings reveal how AGO4/6/9-mediated DNA methylation and AGO5, likely acting through post-transcriptional gene silencing, work together to promote male germline development.

Abstract

Abstract

Himito: a graph-based toolkit for mitochondrial genome analysis using long reads.

▾

Abstract

Understanding the genetic and epigenetic regulation of mitochondrial DNA (mtDNA) is essential for elucidating mechanisms of aging and disease. Long-read sequencing can span the mitochondrial genome and capture base-modification signals, yet analytical tools remain limited. We developed Himito, a graph-based toolkit for analyzing mitochondrial genome using long reads. Himito is available at https://github.com/broadinstitute/Himito .

Cost-efficient long-read trio-barcoded adaptive sequencing improves rare disease diagnosis.
Fu Y, English AC, Paulin LF, …, Sedlazeck FJ
▾

Abstract

Rare diseases often remain unsolved because causal genetic changes can be complex and thus missed by standard sequencing or difficult to prioritize. Long-read sequencing can reveal structural variants, repeat expansions, DNA methylation and inherited haplotypes, but trio sequencing of an affected child and both parents remains costly. Here we show that phenotype-driven Trio-barcoded Oxford Nanopore Adaptive Sequencing (TBAS) enables cost-efficient long-read analysis of rare-disease trios on one flow cell. TBAS workflow uses clinical features to select broad disease-gene panels, barcodes all three family members and enriches these regions during sequencing rather than targeting a known causal locus. Across 13 trios, TBAS recovered all five known diagnoses and prioritized candidates in five of eight unresolved cases.

Functionally, elevation of H2B-K108ac via genetic code expansion and the loss of Rps19bp1 cause significant alterations in the expression of genes associated with mESC differentiation. Furthermore, Rps19bp1 deficiency promotes neural differentiation in mESC-derived teratoma. Our study develops a robust platform for linking specific histone acetylation with chromatin dynamics and cell fate determination, which lays a foundation for future exploration of additional histone PTMs in ESCs.

The chromatin context differently impacts prime editors and base editors and controls the fidelity and purity of base editing.
Wang Q, Zittersteijn H, Wang X, …, Gonçalves MAFV
▾

Abstract

Base editors and prime editors allow changing specific genomic sequences without chromosomal breakage. However, the role of the chromatin environment and its underlying factors on the efficiency, product fidelity, and purity of prime editing versus base editing is unknown. Here, using human cellular systems that monitor the efficiency and fidelity of gene-editing tools at isogenic target sites controlled by specific epigenetic remodeling factors, we report that heterochromatin impinged by the Krüppel-associated box (KRAB)/KRAB-associated protein 1 (KAP-1)/heterochromatin protein-1 (HP-1) axis alone (non-heritable), or in concert with the DNA methyltransferases DNMT3A and DNMT3L (heritable), mostly hinders prime editors over base editors with the extended portions of prime-editing guide RNAs contributing to this outcome. Structural basis for the non-canonical nucleosome remodeling by ATRX.
Wang X, Yan S, Huang Y, …, Chen Y
▾

Abstract

Alpha-thalassemia/mental retardation syndrome X-linked (ATRX) is a sucrose nonfermentable 2 (Snf2)-family ATP-dependent chromatin remodeler essential for chromatin regulation and genome stability, but its remodeling mechanism remains unclear. Here, we report three structures of the human ATRX ATPase domain (ATRXATPase) in its free, nucleosome-bound, and histone-bound states, revealing a non-canonical mechanism of activity regulation and chromatin remodeling. ATRXATPase adopts a structurally primed but autoinhibited conformation that is relieved upon nucleosome binding. Genome-wide analyses further indicate that ATRX associates with active chromatin independently of death domain-associated protein (DAXX) and contributes to chromatin accessibility at intergenic regulatory elements. These findings define ATRX as a mechanistically distinct remodeler that couples disassembly-biased remodeling to histone chaperoning, thereby supporting dynamic chromatin regulation.

Amyloid-mediated RNA uptake by sperm for embryonic delivery.
Mallen WS, Zabello N, Brenner-Morton S, …, Fitzpatrick AWP
▾

Abstract

Seminal fluid (SF) RNA is essential for embryonic development, but the mechanism by which extracellular RNA is transferred from SF to sperm remains unclear. Here, we identify a role for functional amyloid fibrils in SF that bind and traffic RNA to mammalian sperm and, upon fertilization, to the zygote. We show that highly cationic peptides in SF phase separate with RNA to form amyloid fibrils. Cryo-electron tomography (cryo-ET), volume electron microscopy (volume EM), and fluorescence live-cell imaging reveal that these amyloid fibrils are internalized by mammalian sperm. Furthermore, biophysical and in vitro fertilization (IVF) experiments show that amyloid-bound RNA reaches the zygote, where molecular chaperones can control RNA dissociation from amyloid fibrils. Our work uncovers a mechanism for RNA trafficking from SF to the zygote, establishing amyloid fibrils as potential tools for RNA delivery during fertilization.

Emerging frontiers for AI in single-cell genomics.
Mitra S, Li J, Leslie CS
▾

Abstract

We are just over a decade into the single-cell revolution in biology, which has been coupled to the growing use of artificial intelligence (AI) in single-cell genomics. Most early single-cell RNA embedding models and subsequent "foundation models"-representational models pretrained on large single-cell datasets-did not gain wide traction among experimental biologists. Müller M, Kolesnikova O, Doudin M, …, Eustermann S
▾

Abstract

The male-specific lethal (MSL) complex catalyzes global acetylation of histone H4 at lysine 16, a paradigm for understanding how histone acetylation is linked to transcription and disease. However, the dynamic yet specific mechanisms governing the function of MSL histone acetyltransferase (HAT) and other HAT complexes remain largely elusive. These findings uncover molecular principles underlying the specificity and regulation of histone acetylation in transcriptionally active chromatin.

Data analysis of experimentally solved nucleosome arrays reveals heterogeneous features.
Portillo-Ledesma S, Li Z, Franowicz E, Schlick T
▾

Abstract

Atomic-resolution nucleosome array structures have provided invaluable information on eukaryotic genome packing. Although these arrays are often obtained for regular fibers with designed sequences, such information complements in vivo genome organization data and modeling studies. Here, we analyze such systems with a focus on chromatin organization. We highlight both robust and unique features, as well as suitable average values for coarse-grained modeling, such as the relative orientation and positioning of neighboring nucleosomes, and opening of the linker DNA. Our associated web resource (https://www.biomath.nyu.edu/chromstruct/) shares these collected systems and provides tools for their analysis. The heterogeneous features exhibit a wide range of geometrical properties that depend on linker DNA length, core histone protein variants, auxiliary protein binding, and fiber interdigitation. Yet experimental fiber geometries can be reproduced reasonably using average parameters, as demonstrated here by simulations with our mesoscale model for regular fibers and as reported separately for life-like systems with epigenetic marks. Such analysis may also be useful for modular design of chromatin fibers. The structural characterization of these chromatin building blocks provides new insights into the regulation of chromatin architecture and has implications for the modular design of chromatin fibers.

Abstract

Hence, OSCAR is a useful platform to query, browse, analyze, and visualize cell-specific regulatory programs across diverse human tissues and diseases.

Microsatellite expansion redirects chromatin modifiers to confer insect resistance to Bt bioinsecticides.
Ouyang C, Sun K, Wang F, …, Guo Z
▾

Abstract

Microsatellites are highly mutable genomic elements, yet their role in adaptive gene regulation in insects remains poorly understood. Here, we show that a tetranucleotide (ATGT) microsatellite expansion in the promoter of ABCC2, a key Bacillus thuringiensis (Bt) toxin receptor gene, epigenetically silences its expression and confers resistance to Bt Cry1Ac toxin in Plutella xylostella (L.). Nucleic Acids Res · IF 13.1 · 2026-10-19
Exploring the bistable equilibrium of methylated CpG DNA recognition by the MBD2 protein.
Volkenandt S, Belyaeva J, Li T, …, Bagherpoor Helabad M
▾

Abstract

Methyl-CpG binding domain 2 (MBD2) is a critical epigenetic regulator that selectively binds methylated CpG dinucleotides, key marks controlling gene regulation and chromatin organization. Understanding the interactions and conformational dynamics underlying this high selectivity is essential to elucidate MBD2's regulatory role. Here, using extensive classical MD simulations, we explored the formation of the MBD2-mCpG recognition complex. By initially positioning MBD2 one base pair downstream of its target, we observed its transition to the target site within microseconds. Notably, upon binding, MBD2 adopts two distinct stable conformations: a primary state closely resembling the X-ray crystal structure, and a secondary state of reduced affinity that nevertheless retains comparable selectivity for mCpG. Our results establish S189 as a key macro-switch; loss of its interaction with the methylcytosine backbone shifts the equilibrium toward the secondary state. This is corroborated by MD simulations of the S189A mutant, which preferentially adopts the secondary state-like conformation. Complementary NMR experiments confirm that S189A mutation does not alter mCpG selectivity, while fluorescence polarization measurements reveal a reduced binding affinity, consistent with our MD simulations results. Together, these findings indicate that MBD2 binding to methylated CpG involves a bistable equilibrium, providing new insights into how high affinity and adaptability are balanced in epigenetic recognition. In a broader context, our findings suggest that such alternative bound-state equilibria may represent an inherent feature of specific protein-DNA complexes.

Nat Protoc · IF 13.1 · 2026-10-16
Multiplexed single-cell reduced representation bisulfite sequencing for genome-wide DNA methylation profiling.
Liu F, Huang J, Zheng Y, …, Gu H
▾

Abstract

DNA methylation is a fundamental epigenetic modification that regulates gene expression, maintains genomic integrity and defines cellular identity. Conventional bulk sequencing methods, such as reduced-representation bisulfite sequencing (RRBS), have elucidated genome-wide methylation landscapes but do not reveal cell-to-cell variation, limiting insights into methylation heterogeneity during development, differentiation and disease. Here, we present multiplexed single-cell RRBS (Msc-RRBS), a streamlined and scalable protocol for parallel DNA methylation profiling of hundreds of individual cells. All enzymatic reactions, including cell lysis, restriction digestion, end repair and adapter ligation, are carried out in a single microwell, which eliminates the need for buffer exchange and minimizes DNA loss. Short, indexed adapters are used to efficiently remove adapter dimers and enable effective purification of library DNA by magnetic bead clean-up. The workflow supports the generation of sequencing libraries from single cells, pooled cells or sub-nanogram amounts of purified genomic DNA. The full workflow, from cell sorting to sequencing-ready libraries and data analysis, requires ~6 d for one or more 96-well plates. Msc-RRBS thus provides an efficient, reproducible and high-throughput approach for dissecting cell-to-cell DNA methylation heterogeneity across diverse biological and disease contexts. The experiments require basic molecular biology skills, and primary data processing can be performed by someone with limited bioinformatics training. More advanced analyses need additional computational expertise.

Nucleic Acids Res · IF 13.1 · 2026-10-13
Genomic sequence context differentially impacts stepwise oxidation by TET enzymes to shape DNA hydroxymethylation landscapes.
Erlitzki N, Loo CE, Fowler JM, …, Kohli RM
▾

Abstract

Ten-eleven translocation (TET) enzymes reverse gene silencing imposed by 5-methylcytosine (5mC) in mammalian genomes by catalyzing the stepwise oxidation of 5mC to 5-hydroxymethylcytosine (5hmC), 5-formylcytosine (5fC), and 5-carboxycytosine (5caC). 5hmC both acts as an independent epigenetic mark and promotes replication-dependent DNA demethylation by antagonizing maintenance DNA methylation. Oxidation of 5hmC to 5fC/5caC instead marks the committed step in replication-independent DNA demethylation, whereby 5fC/5caC are actively excised and reverted to unmodified cytosine. Although prior work indicates that TET-mediated oxidation is influenced by bases neighboring 5mC, these studies have been unable to resolve more than two of the relevant species-5mC, 5hmC, and 5fC/5caC-at the same time. As 5hmC is the key fulcrum in DNA demethylation, we established a high-throughput enzymology approach to probe these three cytosine states at base resolution simultaneously across hundreds of sites. We reveal distinct context dependencies for 5hmC generation versus decay, with flanking bases differentially impacting 5hmC persistence. Notably, we demonstrate these predictable preferences in duplexed DNA are lost in single-stranded DNA, mechanistically linking these features to TET-mediated base-flipping. Furthermore, analysis of genomic 5hmC patterns uncovers evidence of sequence context bias in cellular 5hmC accumulation, suggesting a role for TET-intrinsic preferences in shaping cellular epigenetic landscapes.

▾

Abstract

Abstract

Primary tumor-derived lactate primes prostate cancer cells for a dormant fate.
Ippolito L, Pranzini E, Gangarossa G, …, Chiarugi P
▾

Abstract

The metastatic outcome depends on the state of disseminated cancer cells (DCCs) leading to overt metastasis or undergoing dormancy. Before distant organ colonization, primary tumor microenvironment may shape the decision between proliferation and dormancy. Here, we identify lactic acid (LA), a highly abundant environmental metabolite at the primary site in prostate cancer (PCa), as a key inducer of dormant state. LA-uploading PCa cells undergo a proliferation delay, associated with stemness, therapy tolerance, and invasiveness through the nuclear receptor subfamily 2 group F member 1 (COUP-TF1/NR2F1) activation. These traits rely on LA-driven accumulation of lipid droplets (LDs), which serve as reservoirs of lipid-derived acetyl moieties essential for histone acetylation. Notably, the epigenetic reader BRD4 is essential for COUP-TF1 expression and for establishing and maintaining dormancy. In metastatic sites, where LA levels are low, dormant features are lost, allowing DCCs to support metastatic growth. Last, targeting LA-mediated LD formation or BRD4 activation prevents dormancy entry, reducing PCa cell adaptability and increasing vulnerability to antiproliferative therapies.

Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-23
Retroviral insertions contributed to the divergence of human and chimpanzee brains.
Gerdes P, Karlsson O, Garza R, …, Jakobsson J
▾

Abstract

Over the past 5 to 7 million years, humans and chimpanzees have diverged in brain size, structure, and cognition despite high conservation of protein-coding genes. However, retroviral infections within host genomes have introduced numerous species-specific regulatory elements. Chimpanzees harbor lineage-specific PTERV1 endogenous retroviruses that are absent in humans, but their role in brain evolution has remained unclear. We performed an epigenomic analysis of PTERV1 insertions in chimpanzee neural organoids and found they are heavily DNA methylated, forming over 150 species-specific heterochromatin domains with the potential to influence gene regulation. We identified a chimpanzee-specific insertion on chromosome 19 that silences the long noncoding RNA LINC00662 through DNA methylation spreading. CRISPR-mediated deletion of this PTERV1 restored LINC00662 expression in chimpanzee induced pluripotent stem cells. We show that LINC00662, a human-specific transcript expressed during brain development, plays a role in neuronal maturation, axon outgrowth, and neural organoid development. These findings reveal a mechanism by which endogenous retroviral insertions contributed to human-chimpanzee brain divergence.
