Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-23
High-throughput droplet-based enzymatic DNA methylome profiling enables single-cell resolution of epigenetic heterogeneity.
Li G, Yang Y, Li Z, …, Ci W
▾

Abstract

DNA methylation is a fundamental epigenetic modification that regulates gene expression, cellular identity, and disease-associated regulatory programs. However, high-throughput single-cell DNA methylation technologies have been limited by DNA degradation, low CpG recovery, and the poor scalability of bisulfite-based methods. Here, we present scEM-seq, a droplet-based single-cell enzymatic methylome sequencing method that integrates barcode ligation within a single emulsification step, followed by postemulsion cytosine-to-uracil conversion. This design substantially reduces DNA loss and enables robust profiling of tens of thousands of single cells per experiment. scEM-seq generates high-quality single-cell methylomes with moderately improved CpG coverage and balanced read utilization compared with other droplet-based microfluidic methods. Applied to peripheral blood mononuclear cells, scEM-seq accurately resolves major immune lineages, and in prostate cancer tissues, it reveals diverse malignant cell states and patient-specific epigenetic and copy number architectures. scEM-seq enables scalable single-cell dissection of epigenetic heterogeneity across diverse biological and disease contexts, broadening the scope of DNA methylation-based studies into cellular regulation and disease.

Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-22
CREBBP loss drives immune evasion by epigenetically silencing STING and MHC-I in small cell lung cancer.
Gao Q, Wei K, Pan D, …, Jia D
▾

Abstract

Most small cell lung cancers (SCLCs) exhibit low immunogenicity and intrinsic resistance to immunotherapy, yet the underlying mechanisms remain poorly understood. Here, we identify CREBBP loss, a frequent genetic alteration in SCLC, as a key suppressor of tumor cell-intrinsic immune responses that drives an immune-cold tumor microenvironment in Rb1/Trp53-driven autochthonous mouse models. Consistently, Crebbp deletion promotes immune escape in syngeneic SCLC models. Mechanistically, Crebbp loss suppresses Sting and major histocompatibility complex class-I expression by reducing histone H3 acetylation. Notably, HDAC inhibition restores histone acetylation, reactivates these silenced genes, and induces tumor cell-intrinsic immune responses. Moreover, pharmacological HDAC inhibition synergizes with immune checkpoint blockade to suppress tumor growth in both Crebbp-mutant and Crebbp-wild-type SCLCs, with a more prominent effect in Crebbp-mutant tumors. Collectively, these findings establish CREBBP loss as a key mechanism of immune evasion and immunotherapy resistance in SCLC, highlighting HDAC inhibition as a promising strategy to enhance immunotherapy efficacy.

Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-22
RanGAP1 coordinates the ER-lamina interface to drive de novo nuclear envelope reassembly in mouse embryos.
Zhang XJ, Zhou CJ, Song CR, …, Liang CG
▾

Abstract

Nuclear envelope (NE) reassembly is essential for the zygote-to-totipotency transition in mammalian embryos. Beyond its classical roles in nuclear transport and maintenance of the RanGTP gradient, the function of RanGAP1 in embryonic NE reassembly has remained elusive. Here, we show that RanGAP1 localizes to the early embryonic NE, driving reassembly via a distinct structural mechanism. Trim-Away-mediated RanGAP1 depletion causes two-cell stage arrest and severe NE defects, including nuclear pore complex (NPC) loss, failed reassembly, and aberrant "fishnet" membrane structures. Mechanistically, RanGAP1 establishes a structural paradigm for endoplasmic reticulum (ER)-lamina coupling by recruiting Lamin A/C, B1, and B2 to the ER for coassembly. The acidic domain and SUMO-1 modification at Lys526 (K526) are essential for the NPC localization of RanGAP1. Thus, RanGAP1 orchestrates nuclear lamina (NL) assembly by targeting lamins to the ER. These findings uncover mechanisms of NE reassembly that are critical for early embryogenesis and provide insights into NE-related developmental disorders.

Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-16
Latent effector capacity governs reversible T cell exhaustion: A theoretical mathematical model of PD-1 blockade response.
Liew AY, Li Y, Dong H
▾

Abstract

T cell exhaustion is often viewed as a terminal state marked by irreversible loss of function during chronic infection and cancer. However, the rapid recovery of antitumor activity in clinical responders after PD-1 blockade suggests that some exhausted T cells retain hidden functional potential. To explain this paradox, we present a theoretical framework that separates latent effector capacity from active effector function. Latent effector capacity represents a history-dependent state reflecting preserved epigenetic accessibility and regulatory readiness, distinct from ongoing gene expression. In this model, PD-1 signaling acts as a reversible mask that suppresses effector function without eliminating the underlying effector capacity. The framework explains preserved chromatin accessibility, rapid reinvigoration after checkpoint blockade, and variable therapeutic responses. By incorporating nonlinear maintenance and erosion of latent effector capacity, the model predicts a bistable system with a point of no return beyond which exhaustion becomes irreversible. These principles may guide development of mechanistically informed biomarkers and artificial intelligence models predicting response to PD-1 therapy.

Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-09
Long-term immunoprophylaxis by TLR9 ligand mediates macrophage-ILC cross-talk against respiratory viral infection.
Tobuse AJ, Kobiyama K, Tsuchida J, …, Ishii KJ
▾

Abstract

Rapid development of host immunity is critical during the emergence of viral outbreaks when vaccines are not yet available. Here, we demonstrate that CpG oligodeoxynucleotide-schizophyllan complex (K3-SPG) induces antigen-independent, long-term protection against lethal respiratory virus infection. A single intranasal administration successfully enhanced host resistance to infection for as long as 100 days posttreatment. Mechanistically, we observed a lung-restricted biphasic innate activation of macrophages and innate lymphoid cells. Early protection was mediated by CD11b+CD11c+ macrophages (DP MΦ) and monocyte-derived interstitial macrophages (IMΦ). Late protection was mediated by innate lymphoid cells that were epigenetically reprogrammed, suggesting that two distinct innate immune subsets contribute to the maintenance of disease tolerance in the lung microenvironment. Deficiency of TLR9 or TNF-α abrogated innate activation and protective immunity. Collectively, these findings demonstrate that adjuvant-induced prophylaxis is a promising approach to enhancing host resistance against respiratory virus infection, involving the biphasic cooperation between innate immune cells in the local tissue.

Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-09
SMCHD1 is a target for gene activation therapy to treat Prader-Willi syndrome.
Iminitoff M, Le Fevre A, Cameron T, …, Blewitt ME
▾

Abstract

Prader-Willi syndrome (PWS) is a neurodevelopmental disorder caused by loss of expression from the active paternal allele at an imprinted cluster on chromosome 15. Current treatments address individual symptoms rather than the underlying cause of disease. Because all patients preserve a normal but epigenetically silenced maternal copy of these genes, activation of this copy offers a potential disease-modifying therapy. Here, we investigated the epigenetic regulator SMCHD1. First, we demonstrated that SMCHD1 represses the PWS locus in both mice and humans, supporting its relevance as a therapeutic target. Second, we found that SMCHD1 represses the entire PWS locus in neural lineages, extending its previously known role beyond only part of the cluster. Deleting Smchd1 after early development in vivo activated PWS genes in disease-relevant mouse tissues including hypothalamus and improved phenotypes in a PWS mouse model. Despite broader genome-wide binding, postearly development targeting of SMCHD1 appeared remarkably safe, supporting its potential for gene activation therapy for PWS.

Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-07
Epigenetic insights into extreme longevity in the world's oldest terrestrial animal, Jonathan.
Vaisvil B, Schmitt DP, Jones A, …, Clark SW
▾

Abstract

Aldabra giant tortoises (Aldabrachelys gigantea) are exceptionally long-lived. We sequenced the genome and methylome of Jonathan, a 194-year-old Aldabra, to explore the molecular basis of his longevity. Relative to other giant tortoises (A. gigantea and Chelonoidis abingdonii), Jonathan has unique gene variants in most aging pathways. Moreover, Jonathan has substantial DNA methylation and methylation entropy changes compared to four other Aldabras ranging in age from a 5-year-old juvenile to older adults. Notably, we found that lower-entropy regions in Jonathan's methylome were enriched for the promoters of genes involved in the mitochondrial electron transport chain, and RNA metabolism. This suggests that high-fidelity transcription of the genes in these pathways may be crucial for long-lived species. Our findings support a model for aging wherein the maintenance of low methylation entropy in gene promoters is coupled to efficient mitochondrial energy production, efficient RNA processing, and efficient genomic repair.

Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-07
Clonal lineage tracing and parallel multiomics profiling reveal transcriptional heterogeneity induced by ARID1A deficiency.
Miyata K, Yang L, Yang Y, …, Maruyama R
▾

Abstract

Transcriptional heterogeneity drives malignant progression, yet how it emerges within tumors remains unclear. By combining genetic perturbation and lineage tracing with parallel single-cell multiomics, we tracked the consequences of ARID1A loss within individual clonal lineages. ARID1A deficiency increased transcriptional variability both within and across clones, recurrently giving rise to diverse transcriptional profiles that did not converge on a common, coordinated pattern. This variability was accompanied by broadened chromatin accessibility at normally silenced loci and depended on residual SMARCA4 activity. Clones in which this variability was prominent showed no growth advantage under standard conditions but were preferentially recovered under specific environmental stresses and during lung colonization, a context dependence consistent with bet-hedging. Our findings suggest that loss of an epigenetic regulator can promote tumor evolution by relaxing transcriptional constraints and widening the range of phenotypes accessible to a clonal lineage, rather than by imposing a single fixed phenotype.

Sci Adv · IF 11.7 · 2026-10-07
Translocation-induced enhancer rewiring reveals cryptic oncogenic circuits in multiple myeloma.
Zheng E, Li Q, Dou X, …, Sun L
▾

Abstract

Immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) enhancer translocations drive multiple myeloma (MM), but routine diagnostics resolve only 32 to 38% of cases. Here, we introduce TransFinder, a multiomic pipeline integrating SMRT long-read sequencing (LRS), Hi-C, H3K27ac ChIP-seq/CUT&Tag, and RNA-seq to systematically resolve cryptic translocations and their hijacked oncogenes by deconvoluting enhancer-promoter neo-loops at rearrangement breakpoints. Applied to MM, TransFinder identified that t(16;22)(q23;q11), a canonical translocation known to activate MAF, also hijacks enhancers to activate RAB36. It further uncovered previously unrecognized t(5;8)(q35;q24) and t(1;22)(q25;q13), which activate MYC and CBX7, respectively, through chromatin topology rewiring. CRISPR-Cas9-engineered t(1;22)(q25;q13) recapitulated CBX7 induction, and pharmacological inhibition of CBX7 suppressed myeloma cell proliferation. Beyond MM, TransFinder identified enhancer hijacking in chronic myeloid leukemia, where t(9;22)(q34;q11) repositioned an enhancer to activate BCR-ABL1, and pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma, where t(2;12)(p24;q24) hijacked an enhancer to activate SDC1. Linking 3D genome architecture to oncogene activation, TransFinder decodes oncogenic structural variants and their trans-activities, establishing clinically actionable dependencies of noncoding drivers in malignancies.

Dev Cell · IF 10.7 · 2026-10-06
Spatially resolved genome architecture in mouse embryonic development.
Ma Y, Gou B, Xu Y, …, Li X
▾

Abstract

Higher-order chromatin structure is linked to cell differentiation and aging, but its native distribution in tissues and role in organismal development remain unclear. Here, we introduce SpaceA, a spatial omics approach for in situ chromatin architecture profiling. Compared with high-throughput chromosome conformation Capture (Hi-C), SpaceA captures substantially more contacts over distances beyond 10 Mb, thereby enhancing detection of higher-order chromatin dynamics. Applied to nine mouse embryonic sections, compartment A/B identity varies spatiotemporally across native tissue environments and undergoes a dramatic shift between E12.5 and E13.5 stages, coinciding with robust transcriptional transitions but not with marked histone modification changes. Furthermore, unlike the prevalent intra-compartment interaction paradigm, the embryonic liver exhibits a distinct, enhanced chromatin condensation between compartments accompanying expression suppression, as validated by microscopy imaging and heterochromatin adhesion feature extraction. Our study establishes a platform for dissecting how tissue-spatial chromatin heterogeneity, particularly via long-range interactions, orchestrates complex biological processes in multicellular organisms.

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-11-03
Rising cell-free DNA level tracks cancer-associated methylation signal and improves multicancer detection.
Basu S, Hiremath P, Rathod N, …, Cantor CR
▾

Abstract

Cancer patients carry more circulating cell-free DNA (ccfDNA)-small DNA fragments shed into the blood-than healthy people. Most of this excess comes not from the tumor itself but from immune cells, suggesting that it would simply dilute the much smaller amount of tumor-derived DNA in a blood sample. Using a study of 1,480 previously untreated participants across 43 hospitals-including patients with 14 cancer types spanning all stages, patients with benign growths, and healthy volunteers-we show the opposite: as total ccfDNA rises, it becomes enriched, not diluted, in fragments carrying cancer-associated DNA methylation signatures, independent of cancer type, disease stage, and a broad panel of demographic, clinical, and technical covariates. In fact, total ccfDNA explains more variance in cancer-associated methylation signal than the disease stage itself. This basic biological finding suggests a coupling between immune cell activity and tumor methylation. It also has a practical implication: we show that rising ccfDNA concentration is associated with higher cancer detection sensitivity, even within disease stages and particularly in early-stage disease-stage I sensitivity varied from 0.55 in the lowest ccfDNA concentration tertile to 0.85 in the highest, and stage II from 0.59 to 0.86. This suggests that ccfDNA concentration should be considered as a stage-independent stratification variable for benchmarking cancer-detection tests. Interestingly, similar behavior was observed in patients with symptomatic benign lesions, suggesting a broader biological response to abnormal tissue growth and not something unique to malignancy.

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-11-03
Epigenetic aging during the COVID-19 pandemic: A prospective longitudinal study with prepandemic baseline data in children, adolescents, and adults.
Schowe AM, Ruks M, Disselkamp CKL, …, Binder EB
▾

Abstract

Psychosocial stress is a risk factor for aging-related diseases. The COVID-19 pandemic placed a psychosocial burden on individuals, potentially increasing the risk for aging-related diseases for which epigenetic clocks are used as early biomarkers. Based on four epigenetic clocks (DunedinPACE, GrimAge2, Horvath, and PedBE), we investigated whether epigenetic aging changed during the pandemic and whether these changes were associated with objective and subjective pandemic-related stressors and preexisting individual risk factors for poorer physical and mental health. We used prospective longitudinal data (n = 1,028), including repeated salivary DNA collection from children (n = 359, MAGE = 9.5 y), adolescents (n = 385, MAGE = 15.5 y), and adults (n = 284, MAGE = 24.3 y). Saliva was collected before the first phase of containment measures in Germany in March 2020 and after the second phase in 2021. We observed significant increases in epigenetic age acceleration (PedBE: b = 0.17, P < 0.0001; GrimAge2: b = 0.08, P < 0.0001) and pace of aging (DunedinPACE: b = 0.13, P < 0.0001) across all age groups. Prepandemic physical health and parental socioeconomic status, but not objective pandemic stressors, were significantly associated with this change. In adolescents, higher perceived pandemic burden was associated with a larger increase in pace of aging (DunedinPACE: b = 0.04, P = 0.01). Together, these findings suggest that prepandemic individual risk factors for poorer physical and mental health are accompanied by measurable increases in epigenetic aging during the COVID-19 pandemic, with adolescence representing a sensitive phase to its psychosocial burden.

EMBO J · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-31
ATAD2B governs epigenetic silencing of the epithelial program to fuel cancer metastasis and neuronal migration.
Mahesh A, Inayatullah M, Terp MG, …, Tiwari VK
▾

Abstract

Epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition (EMT) is a pivotal developmental program that is frequently co-opted to drive cancer aggressiveness and metastasis. While the dynamic remodelling of histone H3 lysine 27 acetylation (H3K27ac) landscapes is a hallmark of EMT, the specific chromatin readers decoding these epigenetic signals remain largely unknown. Here, we identify ATAD2B as a novel H3K27ac reader essential for cell migration in both cancer metastasis and brain development. Multi-omics profiling revealed that, at EMT onset, ATAD2B targets H3K27ac at active epithelial identity genes via its bromodomain and recruits HDAC1 to epigenetically silence them, enforcing a mesenchymal state. ATAD2B loss impairs migration and invasion in breast cancer lines and Patient-Derived organoids, whereas its overexpression accelerates in vivo lung metastasis and correlates with poor patient survival. Furthermore, ATAD2B promotes the migration of newborn neurons in the developing brain. Our findings establish ATAD2B as a versatile chromatin reader orchestrating cell motility across development and disease.

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-28
Lysine crotonylation acts as an epigenetic switch for glutamate neurotransmission and spatial memory.
Mukherjee U, Basu B, Suryavanshi P, …, Chatterjee S
▾

Abstract

Histone posttranslational modifications, particularly lysine acetylation (Kac), are critical epigenetic regulators of transcriptional programs underlying long-term memory consolidation. Beyond Kac, several other non-acetyl acylations have been identified with the ability to regulate transcription; however, their role in memory consolidation remains unknown. Here, we identify histone lysine crotonylation (Kcr) as a molecular switch for long-term memory and glutamatergic neurotransmission. We find that spatial learning induces distinct spatiotemporal patterns of Kcr across hippocampal subregions. Through genetic manipulations, we show that reducing hippocampal Kcr levels impairs memory, while increasing Kcr levels by means of crotonate administration enhances long-term memory. Single-nuclei transcriptome and chromatin accessibility analyses reveal that crotonate facilitates activation of genes regulating glutamate signaling. Cell-cell communication analysis further suggests a strengthening of glutamatergic signaling within hippocampal excitatory neurons following crotonate administration. Real-time fluorescence imaging with genetically encoded sensors functionally validates our multiomics and computational findings-demonstrating the role of Kcr in regulating presynaptic glutamate release and neuronal activity. In summary, our findings elucidate a key mechanism underlying long-term memory consolidation and excitatory neurotransmission, linking epigenetic events to synaptic function and behavior.

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-28
TEDEdb: A multicohort analysis and resource for transposable element differential expression in cancer.
Calendo G, Chaunzwa M, Dehzangi I, …, Issa JJ
▾

Abstract

The human genome consists of nearly 50% repetitive DNA, referred to for decades as "junk DNA." These repetitive sequences, usually under the strict control of epigenetic silencing, can become aberrantly expressed in cancer. Some of these expressed sequences, e.g., transposable elements (TEs), can induce innate immune responses when derepressed following treatment with epigenetic therapies. As a result, epigenetic therapy has been suggested to augment cancer therapies. TEs are traditionally ignored in most RNA-seq studies and their expression is often excluded from publicly available data sources. Thus, the vast amount of publicly available RNA-seq data is an untapped resource for exploring the role of TE expression in cancer and cancer treatment. Here, we present a uniform reanalysis of over 7,000 RNA-seq samples, encompassing more than 2,000 differential expression experiments across 220 cancer cell lines and 700 drug treatments. We observed that TE expression is more prone to batch effects than gene expression alone, necessitating the use of meta-analysis techniques to probe the dataset for global trends. We confirm that DNMTi and HDACis are powerful inducers of TE expression. We also show that nonepigenetic compounds such as cyclin-dependent kinase and topoisomerase inhibitors can also induce robust up-regulation of TE-derived repetitive DNA elements and confirm that this induction is consistent with transcriptional signatures of viral mimicry response. We made all data available through a publicly available database called the Transposable Element Differential Expression Database, or TEDEdb, and created an interactive web application for its exploration located at https://dataexplorer.coriell.org/TEDEdb/.

EMBO J · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-17
Electrostatic control of chromatin compaction safeguards against apoptotic DNA release.
Spicer MFD, Wijma S, Schütte N, …, Gerlich DW
▾

Abstract

Apoptosis involves extensive intracellular reorganisation to facilitate the clearance of dying cells. A key step in this process is the destruction of the genome through fragmentation by caspase-activated DNase (CAD). Rather than dispersing after CAD-mediated cleavage, DNA fragments are compacted into a dense chromatin compartment, but the underlying mechanism and biological relevance of this compaction remain unknown. Here we show that global deacetylation of histone tails promotes chromatin compaction during apoptosis, preventing DNA release into apoptotic extracellular vesicles. Using synthetic effectors that modulate nucleosome electrostatics independently of histone modifications, we demonstrate that electrostatic attraction alone is sufficient to compact and sequester fragmented chromatin. These findings reveal a mechanism by which global reprogramming of histone modifications coordinates genome fragmentation with its physical sequestration during apoptosis. Our synthetic approach further provides a tool to probe how physical forces contribute to genome organisation across diverse biological contexts.

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-15
Environmental chemical mixtures reprogram mammary epithelial development to epigenetic states associated with breast cancer.
Parrish M, Seraj M, Nikoueian H, …, Kuperwasser C
▾

Abstract

Environmental exposures occur as complex mixtures, yet the mechanisms by which they alter human tissue development and generate cancer-relevant tissue states remain poorly defined. Here, we establish a physiologically relevant 3D human breast organoid high-content screening platform that relates exposure-induced developmental disruption to cancer-relevant cell states. Screening of structurally diverse environmental chemicals as single agents and mixtures reveals heterogeneous but reproducible developmental responses, indicating that distinct environmental chemicals perturb different aspects of mammary morphogenesis. Focusing on bisphenols, we show that a low-dose mixture (BPA, BPS, and BPF; 3BPX) disrupts organoid development and induces transcriptional programs characterized by extracellular matrix remodeling, epithelial plasticity, and partial epithelial-mesenchymal transition, accompanied by widespread DNA methylation remodeling. A 3BPX exposure signature maps to ER+ luminal breast cancers and is preferentially enriched in invasive lobular carcinoma (ILC), a subtype characterized by epithelial plasticity. The 3BPX-associated methylation signature is detectable in primary tumors and adjacent normal tissue and is enriched in ILC. Together, these findings show that environmentally relevant chemical mixtures induce heterogeneous developmental perturbations that converge on conserved transcriptional and epigenetic programs associated with epithelial plasticity and tissue remodeling. These exposure-associated signatures are represented in defined epithelial and stromal compartments in human breast cancers, supporting a model in which developmental exposures may establish field-like, cancer-relevant tissue states.

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-14
Autoimmunity induced by epigenome alteration affecting thymic Treg cell development.
Long J, Huang J, Arai M, …, Sakaguchi S
▾

Abstract

Methyl-CpG-binding domain protein 3 (MBD3), an essential component of the nucleosome remodeling and deacetylase (NuRD) complex, controls chromatin accessibility at lineage-specific transcriptional enhancers and promoters during hematopoietic cell differentiation. Here, we have shown that MBD3 deficiency in the T cell compartment resulted in spontaneous development of T cell-mediated multiorgan autoimmune diseases including fatal autoimmune myocarditis. The MBD3-deficient mice notably showed a significant reduction of CD25+Foxp3+ regulatory T (Treg) cells in both the thymus and periphery without much affecting the development and function of conventional T (Tconv) cells. Mixed bone marrow (BM) chimeras reconstituted with wild-type and MBD3-deficient BM revealed a cell-intrinsic defect in thymic Treg cell lineage differentiation in MBD3-deficient cells. Transcriptional, phenotypic, and functional analyses of thymocyte subsets further revealed that MBD3 deficiency particularly affected the activation of a Treg-specific enhancer of the Il2ra gene encoding CD25, hence impaired CD25 expression in immature CD4 single-positive thymocytes (ImCD4SP) but not in peripheral T cells. The impairment markedly limited thymic Treg precursor cell generation and substantially reduced overall thymic Treg cell output. By contrast, MBD3 deficiency scarcely impacted the TGF-β/IL-2-dependent in vitro Treg cell generation from Tconv cells. Thus, Treg-specific developmental properties such as high IL-2/CD25 dependency of their thymic differentiation may render them more sensitive than Tconv cells to epigenetic changes of the whole T cell compartment. The results provide a mechanistic basis by which general epigenomic alterations in the T cell compartment can be causative of autoimmune and other immunological diseases through selectively affecting thymic Treg cell development.

SETDB1 preferentially silences evolutionarily young retroelements.

▾

Abstract

Transposable elements (TEs) are epigenetically silenced through multiple mechanisms, including heterochromatin formation and DNA methylation. Prior studies have implicated SETDB1 and the Human Silencing Hub (HUSH) complex in heterochromatin formation-based TE repression, but precise characterization of repeat subclass and locus-specific effects for SETDB1 and related mechanisms has been limited by short-read transcriptome mapping. Abstract

Resolution of inflammation is mediated in part by agonists that engage immune cells to dampen inflammatory signaling, promote phagocytic functions, and facilitate tissue repair. G protein-coupled receptor 18 (GPR18) has emerged as an important proresolving receptor that is activated by endogenous mediators including Resolvin D2. 53BP1 condensates regulate DNA replication progression and completion.
Wang F, Geng X, Zhang J, …, Zhang Y
▾

Abstract

DNA replication in mammalian cells proceeds in chromosomal domains during defined windows of S phase; however, the mechanisms coordinating replication with restoration of chromatin states remain incompletely understood. Here, we identify p53-binding protein 1 (53BP1) condensates as regulators of heterochromatin replication and epigenetic restoration. Loss of 53BP1 delays mid-to-late S-phase progression, compromises recruitment of epigenetic restoration factors, and impairs timely completion of heterochromatin replication and restoration of heterochromatin-associated epigenetic marks. Together, our findings reveal a previously unrecognized role for 53BP1 condensates in coordinating heterochromatin replication and epigenetic restoration, while serving as a reservoir for DNA damage response.

Abstract

Myocardial infarction (MI) induces cardiac muscle death and its subsequent replacement by a noncontractile fibrotic scar, which underlies progression to heart failure. Using kenpaullone-derived chemical probes and target-validation approaches, we identified MAP4K4 as a molecular target mediating the prolymphangiogenic effect observed. Finally, in a mouse MI model, kenpaullone enhanced cardiac lymphangiogenesis and improved cardiac function.

Abstract

Abstract

Genome architecture shapes transcriptional responses to topoisomerase depletion in C. elegans.
Morao AK, Chervova A, Kotte A, …, Cecere G
▾

Abstract

DNA supercoiling is an intrinsic consequence of transcription that must be resolved to maintain proper gene expression. How supercoiling shapes transcription dynamics in animal genomes remains unclear. Here, we acutely depleted topoisomerases in Caenorhabditis elegans and applied nascent transcription profiling, nuclear RNA sequencing (RNA-seq), histone modification mapping, and long-read sequencing to capture the transcriptional and chromatin responses to topological stress. We find that topoisomerase depletion preferentially inhibits and uncouples expression of convergent genes, while transcription initiation and coordinated expression of divergent genes are comparatively maintained. Topoisomerase degradation hinders transcription elongation globally, resulting in reduced production of longer transcripts and overall shortening of poly(A) tails. These elongation defects are not accompanied by global changes in elongation-associated histone modifications but coincide with modest reductions in promoter and enhancer marks. Our results indicate that genome architecture shapes transcriptional responses to DNA supercoiling, uncovering a topological basis for coordinated gene expression in a multicellular organism.

Histone H3K27 methylation states are sequentially catalyzed in cycling cells.
Greene JE, Ahmad K, Henikoff S
▾

Abstract

Polycomb domains form at silenced genes and are marked by histone H3 lysine 27 tri-methylation, but it is unclear how this methylation is maintained following DNA replication in proliferating cells. Here, we use CUT&Tag chromatin profiling to track the dynamics of H3K27 methylation in human cells. We find that tri-methylation in Polycomb domains is maintained by stepwise methylation after DNA replication. Outside of Polycomb domains, thousands of transcriptionally inactive genes gain histone H3K27 di-methylation hours after DNA replication. Acute treatment with small-molecule inhibitors of the Polycomb repressive complex 2 (PRC2) histone methyltransferase slows H3K27 methylation during S-phase of the cell cycle and increases acetylation of the residue. This effect is most pronounced at Polycomb domains replicating in early S-phase, thereby suggesting that early replication and slow histone methylation confer plasticity to developmentally silenced genes. Together, our results explain how H3K27 modification patterns are faithfully duplicated in rapidly proliferating cells.

Molecular mechanisms of promoter silencing by transcriptional readthrough.
Shipman GA, Walback E, González AG, …, Majewski J
▾

Abstract

Transcriptional interference is the in cis suppression of one transcriptional process by another. To define the mechanisms by which transcriptional readthrough is converted into stable gene silencing, we develop a mouse cell line model of cobalamin C deficiency associated with MMACHC silencing (epi-cblC) by deleting the Prdx1 intron 5 splice acceptor, thereby inducing antisense transcription across Mmachc. Through systematic perturbations of transcription and the H3K36 and DNA methylation machinery, we demonstrate that antisense transcription is the primary driver of repression, whereas H3K36me3 contributes directly to promoter silencing and is further required for the establishment of DNA methylation, which in turn confers long-term stability. Together, these findings define a hierarchical silencing cascade by which transcriptional readthrough establishes stable gene repression and demonstrate that transcription itself can direct local chromatin remodeling and changes in gene expression.

Multiplexed CRISPRi reveals a transcriptional switch between KLF activators and repressors in the maturing neocortex.
Kirk RW, Sun L, Xiao R, …, Nelson SB
▾

Abstract

A critical phase of mammalian brain development takes place after birth. Neurons of the mouse neocortex undergo dramatic changes in their morphology, physiology, and synaptic connections during the first postnatal month, while properties of immature neurons, such as the capacity for robust axon outgrowth, are lost. The genetic and epigenetic programs controlling prenatal development are well studied, but our understanding of the transcriptional mechanisms that regulate postnatal neuronal maturation is comparatively lacking. By integrating chromatin accessibility and gene expression data from two subtypes of neocortical pyramidal neurons in the neonatal and maturing brain, we predicted a role for the Krüppel-like factor (KLF) family of transcription factors in the developmental regulation of neonatally expressed genes. We demonstrate that this switch is buffered by redundancy between KLF paralogs, which our multiplexed CRISPRi strategy is equipped to overcome and study. Our results indicate that competition between activators and repressors within the KLF family regulates a conserved component of the postnatal maturation program that may underlie the loss of intrinsic axon growth in maturing neurons. This could facilitate the transition from axon growth to synaptic refinement required to stabilize mature circuits.

Abstract

Surprisingly, the target genes of LINC00847 (transcribed from Chromosome 22) are substantially enriched on Chromosome 19, with a preponderance of target genes encoding RNA metabolism and RNA splicing factors. Collectively, our study provides a resource to discern artifacts in the doxycycline-inducible system and identifies temporally regulated targets of six lncRNAs for future mechanistic studies.

Abstract

The Mendelian disorders of the epigenetic machinery (MDEMs) are an emerging group of disorders which commonly involve disruption of intellect and growth. To investigate consequences of epigenetic machinery (EM) disruption during neurodevelopment, we systematically knocked out (KO) EM factors in neuronal progenitors isolated from the murine hippocampus and established a neurodifferentiation model to interrogate their functions. While Dnmt1-KO induces extensive DNAm alterations, Kmt2a-KO has little effect on methylation. Nevertheless, the disruption of Kmt2a and Dnmt1 produces convergent transcriptional changes. Loss of either EM factor leads to premature neuronal differentiation, partially explaining this convergence, and MYC emerges as a shared regulatory node linked to downregulation of cell cycle programs in these cells. Extending our methylation analysis to 46 EM genes, we find that loss of DNA methyltransferases induces the strongest DNAm changes, whereas other EM-KOs have subtle or negligible effects. However, clustering of EM-KOs based on promoter DNAm levels reveals three distinct EM subgroups, of which two are enriched for interactions with the DNAm machinery. Allele-specific analysis of DNAm further identifies a single differentially methylated region shared across the 46 EM-KOs, localized to the B6J allele over the Zic4 3'UTR. Furthermore, Zic4 overexpression appears to maintain the neuronal progenitor state, suggesting functional relevance of this locus. Taken together, our results reveal both gene-specific and convergent transcriptional effects across diverse EM-KOs and provide novel insights into the downstream consequences of EM dysfunction.

HSAT3 lncRNA regulates replication re-initiation and colocalizes with TOP2A under stress conditions.
Kanne J, Heß F, Min W, …, Schweiger MR
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: Long non-coding RNAs transcribed from pericentromeric Satellite 3 repeats (HSAT3) are involved in stress-dependent splicing processes and maintenance of genome stability. HSAT3 expression is induced upon several cellular stressors and builds nuclear stress bodies (nSB), which recruit a large number of RNA-binding and regulatory proteins. Recently, we reported that HSAT3 transcripts induce resistance against the topoisomerase 2a (TOP2A) inhibitor etoposide in lung cancer. Epigenetics Chromatin · IF 4.2 · 2026-10-22
Apparent methylation of mitochondrial DNA in WGBS is dominated by technical artifacts.
Yin JJ, Gong ZC, Mao SQ
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: DNA methylation is a critical epigenetic modification that occurs on cytosine residues and plays essential roles in regulating gene expression, cellular differentiation, and genomic imprinting. However, the existence of cytosine methylation in mitochondrial DNA (mtDNA) remains highly controversial. Whole-genome bisulfite sequencing (WGBS) has been widely used to assess mtDNA methylation, but signals detected in mitochondrial DNA are frequently influenced by technical artifacts. RESULTS: We systematically evaluated technical factors that influence mtDNA methylation measurements using zebrafish WGBS datasets derived from a wide range of tissues and experimental conditions, complemented by analyses of human and mouse datasets. mtDNA methylation measurements varied widely among samples within CpG, CHG, and CHH contexts, and mtDNA CpG methylation estimates above 2% were often accompanied by comparable CHG and CHH methylation estimates, even when lambda DNA CpG methylation remained below 2%. Notably, samples lacking documented DNA fragmentation tended to show substantially elevated mtDNA methylation compared with those subjected to fragmentation. Although strand-specific sequencing biases were common, they were not a sufficient cause of elevated false-positive methylation signals. The degree of strand coverage imbalance did not predict high methylation in datasets with documented fragmentation, and high methylation in undocumented samples was preferentially observed where strand coverage was balanced rather than imbalanced. Kit annotations were associated with dataset-level variation in mtDNA methylation, particularly for library preparation kits, but elevated signals were not confined to any single bisulfite conversion or library preparation kit. Cross-species analyses showed that human and mouse datasets displayed comparable patterns of elevated mtDNA methylation. CONCLUSIONS: Our findings indicate that high mtDNA methylation signals detected in WGBS data are dominated by technical artifacts, particularly DNA fragmentation status and bisulfite conversion efficiency. These results highlight the need for careful interpretation of mitochondrial methylation signals and provide a framework for distinguishing technical noise from potential biological signals in WGBS-based analyses.

Abstract

While 5-methylcytosine (5mC) DNA methylation is a well-known player in genome stability and transcriptional regulation, the role of 5-hydroxymethylcytosine (5hmC), particularly prevalent in neurons, remains largely unknown. Antony S, Devaiah BN, Konaté MM, …, Doroshow JH

Abstract

Epigenetic dysregulation and reactive oxygen species (ROS) contribute to melanoma progression and therapeutic resistance, but their interplay in uveal melanoma (UM) remains incompletely understood. However, the lack of a well-established selective NOX5 inhibitor presents a challenge for therapeutically exploiting NOX5 suppression. Conversely, combining Tacedinaline with the superoxide dismutase 1 (SOD1) inhibitor LCS1 significantly enhanced growth inhibition compared with either agent alone. These findings identify ROS modulation in either direction as a potential UM therapeutic strategy, while highlighting pharmacological ROS elevation through HDAC inhibition and disruption of antioxidant defenses as a potentially more practical approach to exploiting NOX5-driven oxidative vulnerability.

Brzozowski TL, Vincent MC, Keri RA
▾

Abstract

Ribonucleotide reductase (RNR) is the rate-limiting enzyme in deoxyribonucleotide synthesis and is therefore essential for DNA replication, repair, and cell-cycle progression. Because cancer cells rely heavily on nucleotide production to sustain proliferation and genome maintenance, RNR has long been an important therapeutic target. However, while several RNR inhibitors are clinically approved, their utility can be impacted by dose-limiting toxicities, poor pharmacokinetic and pharmacodynamic properties, and diverse mechanisms of therapeutic resistance. In this review, we examine emerging mechanisms that regulate RNR in cancer beyond canonical cell-cycle and DNA-damage pathways that could serve as additional targets for blocking RNR. We summarize mechanisms underlying resistance to conventional RNR inhibitors, including altered drug transport and metabolism, RNR overexpression, and acquisition of stem-like and EMT phenotypes with an emphasis on approaches to overcome these limitations. Lastly, we provide an in-depth summary of preclinical RNR inhibitors, highlighting how they compare to FDA-approved RNR inhibitors and their progression in clinical trials. Collectively, these advances underscore the role of RNR in tumorigenesis and identify opportunities to develop more selective and effective RNR-targeted therapies.

Abstract

Activating mutations in PIK3CA (encoding the p110α kinase) are among the most frequent oncogenic drivers in human tumors. Abstract

Notably, these core DMRs are not covered by the probes on the methylation arrays (450K and EPIC v2), highlighting the advantage of WGBS for novel biomarker discovery. In conclusion, tissue-plasma concordance analysis identified a panel of plasma cfDNA methylation biomarkers with strong diagnostic potential. The resulting RF model provides a promising non-invasive strategy for GC screening.

J Biol Chem · IF 4 · 2026-10-13
Differential histone tail citrullination by protein arginine deiminases observed via NMR spectroscopy.
Kowalczyk AJ, Morrison EA
▾

Abstract

Citrullination is a charge-modifying post-translational modification whereby proteinogenic arginine is converted to the non-coded amino acid citrulline by calcium-activated protein arginine deiminases (PADs; EC 3.5.3.15). The five known PAD enzymes in humans (PADs 1, 2, 3, 4, and 6) are differentially expressed and have distinct protein targets, including histones. While some PAD histone citrullination sites are known, a comprehensive investigation of all histone tail arginines targeted by catalytically active PADs 1-4 is lacking. Here, we sought to identify PAD citrullination sites in histone tails, within both histone peptides and reconstituted nucleosomes. Toward this objective, we utilized a real-time 1H-15N NMR spectroscopy-based assay. By monitoring both arginine and citrulline backbone amide peak intensities over time, we identified sites of citrullination in 15N-labeled histone tails within peptides and reconstituted nucleosome core particles. We found that PADs 1, 2, and 4 citrullinate all directly observable histone tail arginines to varying degrees. This is distinct from PAD3, which only moderately citrullinates H2A and H4 arginine residues and does not modify H3 tail arginines. Together, these data suggest a level of histone arginine specificity by each PAD. Furthermore, histone tail citrullination is altered within nucleosomes as compared to isolated peptides, which we interpret as reflecting differences in conformation and accessibility. We also speculate that citrullination increases nucleosomal histone tail dynamics, with implications for crosstalk between sites of histone citrullination and other important sites of regulation by PTMs (including lysines) within and between tails.

Abstract

In animals, plants, and some fungi, Polycomb Repressive Complex 2 (PRC2) catalyzes trimethylation of histone H3 lysine 27 (H3K27me3) to establish transcriptionally repressed chromatin. Here, we identify the histone acetyltransferase RTT109 as a key regulator of PRC2-repressed domains in the model fungus Neurospora crassa. Abstract

In mammals, the first cell fate decisions segregate pluripotent fetal cells from the differentiating extraembryonic lineages. In spite of the unique SOX2 expression pattern, however, the first cell fate decision proceeds normally in the absence of Sox2, suggesting an undiscovered compensatory mechanism. CpG site- and methylation level-dependent measurement variability across three bisulfite-based DNA methylation assays.
Rangani F, Needhamsen M, Kular L, …, Pahlevan Kakhki M
▾

Abstract

Accurate quantification of DNA methylation at specific CpG sites is essential for epigenetic studies, yet measurements can vary depending on assay design and experimental context. Here, we examined locus-specific DNA methylation quantification across three widely used bisulfite-based assays, Illumina Infinium MethylationEPIC array, QIAseq targeted methyl sequencing (TMS), and pyrosequencing, using identical DNA samples. This controlled framework enabled evaluation of method-dependent measurement behavior across CpG sites and a range of methylation levels while minimizing biological variability. Across shared CpGs, all three platforms consistently captured relative methylation trends; however, absolute methylation values differed systematically in a CpG- and methylation-dependent manner. Pyrosequencing generally reported higher methylation levels compared with TMS, whereas EPIC array measurements showed increased variability at low methylation levels (<10%), resulting in reduced inter-method agreement in this range. In contrast, concordance between methods improved at moderate-to-high methylation levels. Error estimates and agreement analyses further indicated that measurement variability was not uniform across CpGs, highlighting site-specific and methylation-level-dependent effects. Together, these results demonstrate that locus-specific DNA methylation quantification using bisulfite-based assays is influenced by local CpG context and methylation level. While relative methylation patterns are preserved across platforms, absolute values are not directly interchangeable, particularly at low methylation levels. These findings underscore the importance of context-aware interpretation and orthogonal validation when precise, locus-specific DNA methylation quantification is required.

We examined longitudinal DNA methylation changes in 10 adults with major depressive disorder undergoing ketamine-assisted psychotherapy using two pre-treatment assessments (T1, T2) and one post-treatment assessment (T3). Participants received three sessions of intranasal racemic ketamine within a structured psychotherapy protocol. Buccal DNA methylation was measured using the Illumina MethylationEPIC v2.0 BeadChip. Depressive symptoms improved markedly following treatment, with mean Beck Depression Inventory-II scores declining from the severe to the mild range (p < .001). Of 802,595 CpG loci evaluated, 20,770 met the nominal T3-versus-T2 screening criterion. Sequential filtering for methylation-change magnitude and movement relative to pre-treatment fluctuation identified 962 retained CpG loci, including 517 retained gene-annotated loci. A supplementary linear mixed-effects analysis modeled repeated measurements within participants across the 603 preselected gene-annotated magnitude-filtered loci; because these loci were selected using the same contrast and cohort, this analysis was not treated as independent confirmation. Exploratory enrichment analysis of this 603-locus set identified NOTCH1-related Reactome terms driven by NOTCH1, DLL1, and ADAM17. Within the retained signature, multiple core and adjacent Notch-related components formed a coherent signaling module spanning ligand, receptor processing, transcriptional regulation, and pathway cross-talk. These findings identify a structured peripheral methylation signature associated with ketamine-assisted psychotherapy. They do not establish brain-specific pathway activation or causality, but provide a longitudinal basis for investigating regulatory adaptations accompanying clinical improvement.

PLoS One · IF 2.9 · 2026-10-14
Personalized genomic landscape of prostate cancer in African American men reveals novel drivers and therapeutic opportunities.
Murthy S, Fago K, Knight J, …, Jung M
▾

Abstract

African American (AA) men experience disproportionately high prostate cancer incidence and mortality. Despite this disparity, current genomic repositories are predominantly derived from European populations, and studies using archived tissues are often limited by stromal contamination. We sought to characterize the high-resolution somatic mutational landscape of AA prostate cancer using a high-fidelity, patient-matched model system to identify population-specific drivers and therapeutic targets. We performed whole-genome sequencing (WGS) on 10 matched pairs of primary cell cultures derived from prostate tumors and adjacent non-malignant tissues of AA patients. Our analyses revealed a complex, heterogeneous landscape characterized by significant genomic instability, including frequent chromothripsis and kataegis signatures. Mutation hotspots were concentrated on chromosomes 1, 2, 3, and X, with the highest mutation density on chromosome 17. Functionally impactful mutations were identified in critical oncogenic pathways, including chromatin remodeling (CHD1, ARID1A, ATRX, KDM6A) and DNA damage response (ATM, BRCA1/2, RAD51). Additionally, we identified potentially novel candidate mutations unique to this AA cohort, distinct from those in European-centric datasets. This study provides a foundational, patient-specific WGS dataset derived from African American prostate cancer cell models. The identification of high-frequency actionable alterations in epigenetic and DNA repair pathways highlights the utility of this renewable resource for developing tailored therapeutic interventions and addressing racial disparities in prostate cancer outcomes.

PLoS One · IF 2.9 · 2026-10-09
Transcriptomic analysis of zebrafish (Danio rerio) liver exposed to sediments from the Loa River.
Lobos-Parra N, Leiva NV, Larama G, …, Orrego R
▾

Abstract

Metal(loid) contamination in aquatic ecosystems, particularly the Loa River in Chile, poses significant environmental and health challenges. This study investigated the molecular impacts of such contamination by exposing zebrafish (Danio rerio) to sediments from pre-impact and severely impacted zones of the Loa River, utilizing transcriptomic analysis of zebrafish liver. Sediment characterization confirmed arsenic as the main driver of contamination, with As concentrations significantly higher at La Finca. Our findings reveal a clear and distinct cascade of molecular responses in fish exposed to impacted sediments, which provides evidence for an Adverse Outcome Pathway. This AOP commences with Molecular Initiating Events, including significant differential expression of genes related to metal adsorption and homeostasis (slc31a1, slc40a1; FDR-adjusted q < 0.05; p < 0.05 for IT7 vs C0 and IT7 vs PIT7), signifying direct perturbations in intracellular metal regulation. This is followed by Key Events characterized by heightened cellular stress, evidenced by significant upregulation of xbp1, xbp1a, xbp1b, and hsp90a/hspd1 (IT7 vs C0; p < 0.05), consistent with oxidative stress and endoplasmic reticulum dysfunction, as well as hypoxia-like conditions. Progressing further, significant differential expression of angiogenesis-related genes (ptprb, mib1, adam10a, foxc1a; p < 0.05) suggests activation of tissue remodeling processes. The cascade culminates in alterations of genes associated with early carcinogenic signaling, including epigenetic regulation (hdac1) and genomic instability (rassf1, top2b; FDR q < 0.05; p < 0.05). This research demonstrates the power of transcriptomics to differentiate the biological impact of contaminated sediments from pre-impact conditions, offering a detailed molecular fingerprint of sublethal effects crucial for identifying early warning signals of environmental degradation and understanding potential human health impacts.

PLoS One · IF 2.9 · 2026-10-07
Integrative methylome and transcriptome analyses identify LHX3 as an epigenetically downregulated marker with tumor suppressive properties in breast cancer.
Farooq K, Gull S, Farooq Z, …, Shahzad N
▾

Abstract

Breast cancer (BC) remains a major global health concern, with rising incidence and mortality rates. Early diagnosis of BC plays an important role in its prevention. However, number of clinically validated biomarkers that enable accurate and minimally invasive diagnosis are still insufficient. Epigenetic modifications, particularly hypermethylation-mediated inactivation of numerous tumor suppressor genes occur early in tumorigenesis and are detectable even before the clinical onset, making them promising candidates for early diagnosis. Therefore, this study aimed to identify breast cancer-associated epigenetically regulated genes with potential diagnostic and biological relevance. A set of forty genes that displayed both hypermethylation and downregulation in BC were initially identified through bioinformatics analysis of publicly available datasets. Based on Gene Ontology (GO) and Reactome pathway enrichment analyses, eight genes were subsequently shortlisted for experimental analysis. Among these eight genes, LHX3 was significantly downregulated in both MCF-7 and MDA-MB-231 cells and in all tested BC tissue samples. LHX3 promoter hypermethylation was found in 80% of tumor tissues, compared to 32% in matched normal tissues. Aberrant LHX3 promoter methylation was significantly associated with metastasis status (P = 0.0002), with higher methylation in non-metastatic tumors, tumor size ≤20 mm (P = 0.002), and advanced tumor stage (P = 0.005). Given the complex pattern of these associations, findings were interpreted cautiously. Treatment with 5-aza-2'-deoxycytidine reversed promoter hypermethylation and restored LHX3 expression in MDA-MB-231 and MCF-7 cells. Additionally, ectopic expression of LHX3 not only reduced migration and proliferation but also induced cell death in BC cells. Our study provided the first evidence of epigenetically induced silencing of LHX3 and its anti-tumor role in BC. Furthermore, our findings support LHX3 as a candidate epigenetically regulated biomarker with tumor-suppressive properties in BC; however, independent cohort validation, quantitative methylation assays, and stage/subtype-stratified analyses are required before its diagnostic or prognostic utility can be established.

PLoS One · IF 2.9 · 2026-10-06
CfDNA methylation profiling in blood following moderate-to-severe traumatic brain injury using brain cell type-enriched signatures: A pilot study.
Kao SH, Singh LN, Starr J, …, Kilbaugh TJ
▾

Abstract

The human brain consists of diverse cell types that collaboratively perform complex functions and form intricate neuronal networks. When traumatic brain injury (TBI) occurs, multiple molecular pathways involving diverse cell types are activated in response to injury. Understanding how brain cell composition is altered during TBI may help improve patient outcomes. Upon brain trauma, neurovascular disruption leads to the release of brain-derived cell-free DNA (cfDNA) into peripheral blood. The tissue-specific DNA methylation patterns in this cfDNA have the potential to serve as candidate markers for studying brain injury. To characterize brain cell type-enriched methylation features, we analyzed the methylomes of five major brain cell types-neurons, astrocytes, oligodendrocytes, microglia, and vascular endothelial cells (VECs)-using whole-genome bisulfite sequencing (WGBS). Cellular enrichment analysis showed that differentially methylated genes (DMGs) exhibited reference-defined cell type-enriched patterns. Gene Ontology pathway analysis further supported the biological plausibility of these methylation features. To demonstrate the feasibility of applying cfDNA methylation profiling in TBI, we analyzed a limited pilot dataset comprising healthy controls and samples collected at acute (1 day), subacute (1 month), and chronic (6 months) post-injury time points from patients with moderate-to-severe TBI (msTBI). These analyses identified candidate brain cell type-enriched cfDNA methylation features that differed across post-injury sampling groups. Together, our findings highlight that brain cell type-enriched methylation signatures may provide a powerful tool for characterizing distinct cell populations and nominating candidate brain cell-associated cfDNA methylation biomarkers in TBI.

PLoS One · IF 2.9 · 2026-10-06
Optimised fluorescence-activated nuclei sorting for epigenomic analysis of cortical cell types.
Chioza B, Policicchio S, Burrage J, …, Dempster EL
▾

Abstract

Increased understanding of the functional complexity of the genome has led to growing recognition of the role of non-sequence-based regulatory variation in disorders of the human central nervous system. Most genomic analyses of the brain are limited by the use of bulk tissue, which comprises a heterogeneous mix of different neural cell types with distinct epigenetic profiles, thereby limiting the ability to attribute regulatory changes to specific cell populations. Given the limited availability of human post-mortem tissue resources and the importance of integrating multi-omic data from the same samples, there is a critical need for methods that enable parallel, cell-type-resolved genomic profiling. We present optimised protocols using fluorescence-activated nuclei sorting (FANS) to isolate nuclei from different human and mouse brain cell types for downstream multi-omic analysis. Our approach enables the robust purification of neuronal, oligodendrocyte, microglial and other glial-origin nuclei from both adult and fetal brain tissue. We demonstrate that FANS-isolated nuclei are compatible with a wide range of genomic assays, including profiling of DNA modifications, histone modifications, chromatin accessibility, and gene expression. This protocol maximises the utility of limited post-mortem tissue resources and provides a unified workflow for comprehensive, cell-type-specific interrogation of molecular mechanisms involved in the brain.

Biol Open · IF 2.3 · 2026-10-12
Nuclear blebs are composed of variable chromatin states and display increased transcription initiation relative to elongation.
Clark M, Losada A, Jahng S, …, Stephens AD
▾

Abstract

Nuclear blebs are herniations of the nucleus that occur in many human conditions including aging, heart disease, muscular dystrophy, and many cancers. Nuclear blebbing causes nuclear rupture and cellular dysfunction. However, understanding the formation, stability, and identification of nuclear blebs remains an ongoing challenge. Our previous studies revealed that nuclear blebs are best hallmarked by decreased DNA density. To determine if chromatin decompaction underlies decreased DNA density in nuclear blebs, we investigated the histone composition of nuclear blebs across multiple cell lines. Time-lapse and immunofluorescence imaging revealed that histone levels decreased in the nuclear bleb compared to the nuclear body but were more variable than DNA. Next, we imaged histone modification states of euchromatin and heterochromatin, which track the decondensed and compact states of chromatin, respectively. We found that nuclear blebs displayed variable histone modification states across cell lines, as euchromatin is not consistently enriched nor is heterochromatin consistently depleted. Nuclear blebs did consistently show that active RNA polymerase II initiation is significantly increased relative to elongation. Thus, decreased DNA density that hallmarks nuclear blebs is not due to local histone modification state, and the increase in transcription initiation relative to elongation is reproducible in nuclear blebs.
